
===== 001 BEGIN Execution code-brief.md | 3204 bytes | SHA256 96016869f1735c244387bd7c78c2da4e26c330952f446604732f77944b2b1e64 =====
# CODE review: bounded complete-buffer BAO proof

Review the additive `cyber_bao::proof` API on exact Radio parent
b933829cbe273d2f31fe75ca83125f3ddd8917a2. The frozen PLAN was approved by a fresh
Opus session. This CODE review sees the actual implementation and its tests;
PLAN approval is separate. Return APPROVE or REQUEST CHANGES with concrete blockers
separated from finite advice. No source edits, commit or merge by the reviewer.

The three production changes are one module declaration and two new source files.
They compose the merged RangeTraversal and GroupHasher without changing kernels,
legacy readers, dependencies or features. This is an additive minor API reaching
Radio's soft3 rung; it is not release acceptance or complete foundation row 4.
The future Kadek adapter reaches Cyb separately and is outside this implementation.
No license, canon or specification text changes are included. Public Rustdoc is
the new carrier, error, work, output and lifetime contract.

Trust is conditional on the supplied complete root and size. Canonical field-word
checks precede arithmetic, exact EOF alone seals the immutable borrowed result,
and output replays one geometry item per call. Whole selected groups can extend
outside the request. The future Kadek adapter must retain its BaoAnchor association,
charge verification/output/copy and own bounded storage before publishing ranges.
This API authenticates neither a caller-invented particle association nor a first
partial download. No network, serialized anchor or generic budget is added.

Review targets: canonical-word order/offsets, ROOT and absolute chunk counters,
frontier stack correspondence, group completion followed by a later parent,
one bounded action per call, sticky error/strict EOF publication, independent
output coordinates and unforgeable borrowed view. Empty-file verification has four
actions and no output; nonempty-file empty requests are rejected. Geometry admission
is not a promise that a host can allocate a maximal admitted group.

The readable packet includes complete changed sources/tests, full relevant owning
geometry/reducer/backend/carrier and consumer contracts, exact primary excerpts,
approved PLAN/finite cases/review, seal sources and diagnostic classifications,
fixture provenance and gate summaries. Raw command receipts and every source epoch
are in the compact execution audit and independently replayable against exact Git
objects. It includes the real initial invocation/cache incidents and current-parent
negative comparisons; it does not disguise them as successful gates.

Omitted from this bounded review input: bulk locks/metadata, unrelated workspace
runtime/network sources, full prior audit archives, most verbose gate raw output,
compiled targets and the rest of primary repositories outside labeled excerpts.
Their identities remain indexed by the audit and frozen research. The reviewer has
not seen those omitted bodies. Root independently verifies the full raw/source
capsule. Cached registry and external consumer inventory are historical observations,
not current whole-product closure or hosted CI success. No 32-bit runtime or
allocator-free no_std final-link claim is made.

===== 001 END =====

===== 002 BEGIN Execution acceptance-map.md | 2405 bytes | SHA256 57b76f6b345680fc421cb4a41f241a493c610f7e7a85d1713e0bafa85412c73e =====
# Finite acceptance map

All assertions are source-bound; expected roots, offsets, schedules and tree shape
precede the implementation. The initial 17 materialized seed carriers total 107086
bytes. Sixteen are valid finite carriers; the last is a synthetic maximum-depth
prefix, not a witnessed complete huge file. Malformed runtime variants are explicit
mutations in the full test source, not separately materialized in that seed count.

- `proof.rs`: literal schedules, whole-group coordinates, request intersection,
  pointer identity, two independent cursors, terminal output, external quotas,
  total exhaustion, cancellation, interleaved jobs, view/cursor retention and Debug.
- `proof_failures.rs`: geometry/error precedence, all canonical-word positions,
  short headers, every truncated prefix of selected carriers, parent/leaf errors,
  strict suffix rejection, terminal stickiness, grouped ROOT and absolute counters,
  all BlockSize logs, deepest finite prefix and largest-width setup, exact Display.
- `proof_allocation.rs`: observer positive control, successful verification/output,
  terminal polls and cancellation; EOF failure and constructor geometry failure.
  Other error branches are verified functionally, without an allocator observation
  claim. Fixture allocation and destruction happen outside the observed interval.
- `seals/src/bin/*`: positive runtime control plus private fields E0451, private
  constructor E0624, raw conversion E0277, DeserializeOwned and Deserialize<'static>
  E0277, proof drop E0505, proof mutation E0502 and immutable leaf mutation E0594.
  Exact compiler classes are asserted; generic compile failure alone is insufficient.
- `interleaved-oracle/`: independently assembled four-leaf carrier from committed
  kernels, never a traversal/encoder/verifier. Its parent pair after two payloads
  exercises resumed frontier state and replay offsets. Original adapter manifest's
  73 output actions includes its planned copy work; Radio emits six iterator items.

Negative compiler API absence on b933 is recorded separately from actual legacy
carrier runtime observations on a96d. Original tests-first and intermediate source
freezes are preserved. The final tests-only interleaved witness has its own source
bridge and focused debug/release results; earlier broad counts keep their 17-test
source epoch and are not relabeled as runs of the expanded corpus.

===== 002 END =====

Parent pairs are narrower where named failing-cases; they do not rerun unrelated
passing tests. Five mDNS cases fail on both default and no-default candidate and
parent runs. Compilation/lint/docs/fmt/policy reds remain real gate failures even
when present on the exact parent. Unknown causes are not attributed to memory or
network policy without evidence. Cached offline deny is not a fresh advisory fetch.

The literal CI selectors iroh/iroh-relay fail because those package names do not
select local workspace packages. Explicit cyber-radio/cyber-radio-relay all-feature
native checks verify the actual renamed sources. Broad workspace default/none
runs exercise the local docs/blob/CLI/integration consumers. No external product
closure, Linux/Windows run, 32-bit runtime or final no_std allocator-free link is
claimed. The isolated thumb library succeeds; candidate and parent binaries fail
for the existing Hemera allocator dependency.

Source epochs matter: owner final matrices have 17 new tests (default118, all121,
none45); the later one-line module-order correction has targeted ready checks.
The final interleaved test adds an 18th new test without production edits, and only
its focused checks are new. Original source/test/format/Clippy invocation failures,
the mixed parent/candidate cache incident and harmless cleanup-guard refusal are
retained as historical rows; they are not substituted for current final outcomes.

===== 013 BEGIN Execution interleaved-oracle/fixture_oracle.rs | 2995 bytes | SHA256 e1ec0b572fab8b77b74e4dbde16365243618d1c231edbc32fa9d2b5167412552 =====
// Research fixture assembly from explicit small-tree expressions. Uses only
// committed kernels, never a Bao encoder, traversal or proof verifier.
use cyber_bao::{HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend};
use std::{env, fs, path::Path};

fn hex(bytes: &[u8]) -> String {
    bytes.iter().map(|b| format!("{b:02x}")).collect()
}

fn main() {
    let target = env::args().nth(1).expect("new output directory");
    let target = Path::new(&target);
    fs::create_dir(target).unwrap();
    let data: Vec<u8> = (0..16384)
        .map(|i| ((29 * i + 7 * (i / 4096)) % 251) as u8)
        .collect();
    let b = Poseidon2Backend;
    let l0 = b.chunk_hash(&data[..4096], 0, false);
    let l1 = b.chunk_hash(&data[4096..8192], 1, false);
    let l2 = b.chunk_hash(&data[8192..12288], 2, false);
    let l3 = b.chunk_hash(&data[12288..], 3, false);
    let p01 = b.parent_hash(&l0, &l1, false);
    let p23 = b.parent_hash(&l2, &l3, false);
    let root = b.parent_hash(&p01, &p23, true);
    let particle = hemera::hash(&data);

    let mut wire = (data.len() as u64).to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    for h in [p01, p23, l0, l1] {
        wire.extend_from_slice(h.as_bytes());
    }
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 136);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&data[..8192]);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 8328);
    for h in [l2, l3] {
        wire.extend_from_slice(h.as_bytes());
    }
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 8392);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&data[8192..12288]);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 12488);
    assert_eq!(&wire[136..4232], &data[..4096]);
    assert_eq!(&wire[4232..8328], &data[4096..8192]);
    assert_eq!(&wire[8392..12488], &data[8192..12288]);

    let mut selected = Vec::new();
    selected.extend_from_slice(&wire[4231..4232]);
    selected.extend_from_slice(&wire[4232..8328]);
    selected.extend_from_slice(&wire[8392..8393]);
    assert_eq!(selected, data[4095..8193]);
    assert_eq!(selected.len(), 4098);
    // A contiguous slice of the proof carrier includes the intervening pair,
    // and demonstrably is not the requested file range.
    assert_ne!(&wire[4231..4231 + selected.len()], selected.as_slice());

    fs::write(target.join("body.bin"), &data).unwrap();
    fs::write(target.join("interleaved.bao"), &wire).unwrap();
    fs::write(target.join("selected.bin"), &selected).unwrap();
    let manifest = format!(
        "{{\n  \"file_len\": 16384,\n  \"request\": [4095, 8193],\n  \"group_log\": 0,\n  \"particle\": \"{}\",\n  \"root\": \"{}\",\n  \"wire_bytes\": 12488,\n  \"groups\": [[136, 0, 4096], [4232, 4096, 4096], [8392, 8192, 4096]],\n  \"parent_offsets\": [8, 72, 8328],\n  \"derived_verification_actions\": 11,\n  \"derived_output_actions\": 73,\n  \"derived_total_actions\": 84\n}}\n",
        hex(particle.as_bytes()),
        hex(root.as_bytes())
    );
    fs::write(target.join("manifest.json"), &manifest).unwrap();
    print!("{manifest}");
    println!(
        "Explicit small-tree assembly PASS; no Bao traversal/encoder/verifier or Kadek adapter executed"
    );
}

===== 013 END =====

===== 014 BEGIN Execution plan-review/result.md | 10624 bytes | SHA256 834848c396abf1d459d9dec1b98ce6f2c7264b6990006d0e89ad1bf55f4637fb =====
# PLAN Review — bounded whole-buffer Poseidon2 BAO proof verifier

**Verdict: APPROVE**

Reviewed from the supplied frozen brief only: `plan.md`, `finite-cases.md`, `research.md`, archive README, measurement/bridge/fixture/input manifests, both probe sources, all raw stdout/stderr and command receipts, the merged `traversal.rs` and its two test files, parent `group.rs`/`hash.rs`/`tree.rs`/`io/slice.rs`/`root_policy.rs`/`lib.rs`, the Hemera `field.rs`/`encoding.rs`/`params.rs` and spec/kernel excerpts, Kadek `anchor.rs`/manifest/runtime excerpt, Radio CI/manifest/consumer files, and the prior traversal CODE verdict. I ran no tools and did not read the hash-indexed omitted bodies (historical capsules, bulk lock, Hemera sponge/permutation/tree remainder, compiled targets, full metadata stdout).

## What I verified independently

**The action oracle is arithmetically correct.** `2 + P + L + Σ(2·N_g − 1)` with `Σ N_g = C` gives `2 + P + L + 2C − L = 2 + P + 2C`. Each fixture row checks out against the measured `expected_actions` in both raw stdouts:

- `hello`: P=0, C=1 → 4 ✓ (H S K E)
- `three-right`: P=1, C=1 → 5 ✓
- `three-interior`: P=2, C=1 → 6 ✓
- `three-boundary`: P=2, C=2 → 8 ✓ (H P P S K S K E)
- `grouped-left`: P=1, C=2 → 2+1+4 = 7 ✓; the single 8192-byte group needs 2·2−1 = 3 kernels, so H P S K K K E = 7 ✓
- `final-short`: log 1, size 8193 → groups {0..8192}, {8192..8193}; P=1, selected group has 1 byte → N=1, C=1 → 5 ✓
- `sole-group`: P=0, one 8192-byte group → 3 kernels, 1+1+3+1 = 6 ✓
- empty: 4 ✓ with the synthetic zero-length ROOT setup.

The bound `P ≤ groups−1 ≤ N−1` and `total ≤ 3N+1 < 2^54` holds since `N ≤ 2^52`. Nothing overflows u64.

**The canonical-wire tightening is grounded in real evidence, not speculation.** Hemera `field.rs:21` keeps the raw `value` in `[0, 2^64)`; `Goldilocks::new` is a bare `const fn` with no reduction; `encoding.rs::bytes_to_cv` calls `Goldilocks::new` on each LE u64 without reducing; `hash_to_bytes` reduces only on *output*. So two distinct 32-byte encodings can produce identical parent output. The primary stdout confirms exactly this: four alias words, `raw_equal=false parent_equal=true legacy_accept=true`, all under the same synthetic root `9fa00cab…`. The research correctly retracts the earlier "constructor reduces" shorthand. The rejection offsets 40/48/56/64 are right: header 8 + left 32 = 40 is the right child's word 0, and the first pair's left words sit at 8+word·8. `research.md` also correctly refuses to call this a particle bypass — no complete file with root `9fa00cab…` is witnessed, and `measurements.json` records `whole_file_synthetic_root_witnessed: false`.

**ROOT/grouping authority is preserved.** `root_policy.rs` already pins `2abf65dd…` as the canonical D8192 ROOT and `11890a7e…` as the non-ROOT internal CV, and already asserts `LeafMismatch { start_chunk: 0 }` for the historical value across `decode`, `decode_slice`, `encode_ranges_validated` and `valid_ranges`. The PLAN's sole-group rejection requirement is consistent with that existing contract, and `grouping_preserves_fixed_tree_at_boundaries` already witnesses root invariance across logs 0/1/2/4. The primary probe re-derives both literals independently (`assert_ne!(root, p01)`).

**The traversal composition is sound.** Merged `traversal.rs` gives exactly one item per `next`, `is_root` only on the first frame, leaf clipping via `size - start` (no `(first+1)*g` overflow), and 53 frames with pop-before-push. The PLAN's 53-slot expected stack mirrors that frontier bound correctly: at most 52 deferred siblings plus one current expectation. `GroupHasher` is one kernel per pending `step`, `2N−1` total, ROOT only on the completing kernel — matching the Group-kernel row. The PLAN's claim that `GroupHasher::new` cannot fail after checked traversal is correct against `group.rs`: `InputTooLarge` needs `len > u64::MAX` (impossible from a usize slice on ≤64-bit), and `CounterOverflow` needs `start + leaves − 1 > u64::MAX`, excluded by `start_chunk ≤ 2^52−1` from traversal geometry. Using an internal assertion rather than a new public variant is the right call.

**Legacy gaps are real and bounded.** `io/slice.rs::decode_slice` confirms every characterized weakness: the single-block shortcut hashes `&slice[cursor..]` with no length check against `declared_size`; there is no EOF/trailing check; `tree.blocks() <= 1` returns before touching the expected stack, and the multi-group path with an empty filtered traversal never pops the root — hence `multi_wrong_root_accepted=true`. The 321-byte debug/release stdouts are byte-identical. These are correctly labeled as existing-API semantics left unchanged.

**Error precedence is complete and consistent.** Constructor Geometry → EmptyRequest → NonCanonicalRoot (ascending word) places all cheap scalar checks before any byte read, and the constructor reads nothing — so the "no successful mode with an unchecked root" invariant holds. Per-stage Truncated-before-canonical and canonical-before-arithmetic is specified in both directions, including the subtle "noncanonical left followed by incomplete right pair → Truncated wins" case. `TrailingBytes { offset: consumed length }` on a matching last group is the one case legacy gets wrong, and `finite-cases.md` tests both the 1-byte suffix and the concatenated-valid-proof variant.

**Scope and authority boundaries hold.** Kadek `anchor.rs` is committed and self-contained: `BaoAnchor` has private fields, only `BaoAnchorJob::pull` constructs it, and it binds particle + len + root + `HemeraFixed4096V1`. Nothing in this PLAN touches it, and the PLAN explicitly denies minting `VerifiedRange` from a Radio view. `kadek-ifac/Cargo.toml` pins `cyber-bao` at a96d with `default-features = false`, so an additive `pub mod proof` under `#![cfg_attr(not(feature = "std"), no_std)]` is reachable there — the ring-1 / minor-semver framing is honest, not inflated. The bridge shows 38/39 unchanged inputs with the single `lib.rs` module-wiring delta and `candidate_traversal_identical: true`.

**Gate discipline is correct.** No inheritance from the traversal gates is claimed; the prior CODE verdict's disclosure of red repository gates (workspace Clippy, nightly docs, cargo-deny, all-features nextest `iroh-gossip` E0599, last-tag no-default exit 100) is carried forward rather than laundered. The PLAN requires exact-parent comparison with identical env/profile and forbids stale-ancestor reds and a gratuitous second matrix. Local `--locked/--offline/jobs=2/incremental=0` is labeled local strengthening, consistent with the receipts. `.config/nextest.toml` `terminate-after = 3` is in the retained support set, satisfying "read CI profile termination from actual config."

## Blockers

None.

## Advice (non-blocking)

1. **`BlockSize` admission crosses an unchecked boundary.** The prior CODE review already flagged that `BaoTree::blocks()`/`shifted()` use unchecked `1u64 << block_size.0`. Your constructor delegates to `RangeTraversal`, so logs up to 51 are admitted, and the PLAN says "No unchecked BaoTree byte helpers are used for large geometry" — good. Make that an explicit rustdoc non-claim on `ProofVerifier::new` too: admitting log 51 here is not a statement that the same `BlockSize` is safe in `tree.rs`.

2. **`ProofItem::Parent` carries no coordinate.** `Leaf` gets `file_offset`/`proof_offset`/`bytes`; `Parent` is a unit variant. A consumer replaying for retransmission or progress accounting cannot tell *which* parent it just consumed, and the error table already materializes `TreeNode` for `ParentMismatch`. Consider `Parent { node: TreeNode, proof_offset: usize }` — it is free (both are already in the cursor), keeps `ProofItem` `Copy`-ish in spirit, and removes the only asymmetry in the output surface. If you keep it unit, document that parents are deliberately opaque and that consumers must count them positionally.

3. **`VerifiedProof::request` returning a cloned `Range<u64>` prevents `Copy`.** Everything else in the view is `Copy` by value. `Range` is not `Copy`; returning `(u64, u64)` or exposing `request_start()`/`request_end()` would let `VerifiedProof` derive `Copy` and remove a borrow-vs-move wart for callers that pass the view into a copy loop. Minor, but decide before freezing names.

4. **Display-string freeze deserves a single oracle test.** `finite-cases.md` lists eight exact strings and says "do not make diagnostic strings the only oracle" — correct. Add the converse guard: one test that asserts all eight strings *and* that `ProofError` implements `core::error::Error` via a `fn core_error<E: core::error::Error>()` witness, exactly as `range_traversal.rs` does. Cheap, and it caught nothing only because the traversal unit already did it.

5. **The 52-level synthetic prefix needs an explicit cost note.** `finite-cases.md` says "compute only those explicit synthetic commitments with zero deferred sibling CVs." That is 52 parent kernels plus one 4096-byte leaf — fine. But the fixture is ~3.4 KB of pairs plus a 4096-byte group, and the test asserts `Truncated` with a *large* `needed`. State the exact expected `needed` value in the table rather than "exact large needed", so the test cannot be written to accept any large number.

6. **`items()` starting its proof cursor at 8 is an unstated invariant coupling.** The replay cursor re-derives geometry and assumes the header occupied exactly bytes 0..8. That is guaranteed by successful verification, but it means `VerifiedProof` must retain enough state to prove it (or assert it). Make the retained private fields explicit in the implementation doc so a later refactor cannot silently let `items()` start from a stale offset.

7. **Allocation observer: exclude fixture *construction* as well as deallocation.** The brief already says to build and retain fixture storage outside observation and exclude later deallocation. `range_traversal_allocation.rs` shows the pattern works; note that the 8328-byte `three-boundary` and 8264-byte `grouped-left` fixtures must be built before `ACTIVE` flips, or the counter will attribute their `Vec` growth to the verifier.

Scope held: three production paths (one `pub mod` line, two new files), four test files, no dependency/feature/lock/wire-producer/kernel change, no consumer migration, no Kadek source change. Nothing here claims a verifier implementation, measured new-API work, an allocator-free final link, first-partial authentication, row-4 closure, or any rung/release acceptance.
===== 014 END =====

===== 018 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/src/lib.rs | 1345 bytes | SHA256 9e82621f1a213a4943c2d8e5b313621ce0e5cee811f86975fe9bff20195c472a =====
//! Poseidon2-BAO: verified streaming with Poseidon2 hash over Goldilocks.
//!
//! This crate provides BAO-style content-verified streaming using the
//! Poseidon2 algebraic hash function. The tree structure
//! and encoding formats are compatible with the original bao specification,
//! differing only in the hash function used.
//!
//! # Architecture
//!
//! - **`tree`**: Pure geometry — tree node indexing, chunk counting, block sizes
//! - **`hash`**: Pluggable hash backend trait with Poseidon2 implementation
//! - **`io`**: Encoding, decoding, outboard creation, and slice extraction

#![cfg_attr(not(feature = "std"), no_std)]

pub mod group;
pub mod hash;
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub mod io;
pub mod proof;
pub mod traversal;
pub mod tree;

pub use hash::{HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend};
pub use tree::{BaoChunk, BaoTree, BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, ChunkNum, TreeNode};

#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub use tree::PostOrderChunkIter;

/// A set of chunk ranges (used for partial downloads, range requests, etc.).
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ChunkRanges = range_collections::RangeSet2<ChunkNum>;

/// Borrowed reference to chunk ranges.
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ChunkRangesRef = range_collections::RangeSetRef<ChunkNum>;

/// A set of byte ranges.
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ByteRanges = range_collections::RangeSet2<u64>;

===== 019 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/src/proof.rs | 11816 bytes | SHA256 82e5aed5b71ab1263d05df7808046a614991ec5daeab2e30b88a12b970c395b7 =====
//! Strict complete-buffer proofs for the canonical fixed-4096 Hemera tree.
//!
//! Concrete Poseidon2 kernels, counters and domains are those of Hemera d5f0a095.
//! A proof is an LE u64 complete length, then selected preorder child pairs and
//! whole group payloads. Every hash is four canonical LE field words. Grouping
//! changes the carrier, not the complete ROOT; historical internal CVs are not
//! roots. Legacy std carrier APIs have their own, unchanged admission behavior.

use core::{fmt, ops::Range};

use hemera::{Hash, OUTPUT_BYTES, OUTPUT_ELEMENTS, field::P};

use crate::{
    BaoChunk, BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend, TreeNode,
    group::GroupHasher,
    traversal::{RangeTraversal, RangeTraversalError},
};

mod output;
pub use output::{ProofItem, ProofItems, VerifiedProof};

static BACKEND: Poseidon2Backend = Poseidon2Backend;
const _: () = assert!(CHUNK_SIZE == 4096 && OUTPUT_BYTES == 32 && OUTPUT_ELEMENTS == 4);
const PAIR_BYTES: usize = 2 * OUTPUT_BYTES;

/// First failed admission or proof action; coordinates name the encoded proof
/// except for TreeNode indexes and absolute file chunk counters.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ProofError {
    Geometry(RangeTraversalError),
    EmptyRequest,
    NonCanonicalRoot { word: u8 },
    Truncated { offset: usize, needed: usize },
    SizeMismatch { expected: u64, declared: u64 },
    NonCanonicalHash { offset: usize },
    ParentMismatch { node: TreeNode },
    LeafMismatch { start_chunk: u64 },
    TrailingBytes { offset: usize },
}

impl fmt::Display for ProofError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Geometry(e) => e.fmt(f),
            Self::EmptyRequest => f.write_str("empty request for nonempty file"),
            Self::NonCanonicalRoot { word } => write!(f, "noncanonical expected root word {word}"),
            Self::Truncated { offset, needed } => {
                write!(f, "truncated proof at {offset}: need {needed} bytes")
            }
            Self::SizeMismatch { expected, declared } => {
                write!(f, "proof size {declared} differs from expected {expected}")
            }
            Self::NonCanonicalHash { offset } => {
                write!(f, "noncanonical proof hash word at {offset}")
            }
            Self::ParentMismatch { node } => write!(f, "proof parent mismatch at node {}", node.0),
            Self::LeafMismatch { start_chunk } => {
                write!(f, "proof leaf mismatch at chunk {start_chunk}")
            }
            Self::TrailingBytes { offset } => write!(f, "trailing proof bytes at {offset}"),
        }
    }
}
impl core::error::Error for ProofError {}

struct Pending<'a> {
    reducer: GroupHasher<'a, Poseidon2Backend>,
    expected: Hash,
    start_chunk: u64,
}

/// Resumable verification against an externally trusted complete root and size.
///
/// A returned view authenticates selected bytes only relative to those supplied
/// inputs. This API establishes no particle/root association or Kadek authority.
/// It borrows the immutable encoded proof, acquires no owner and allocates nothing.
/// Cancellation is dropping the verifier; no byte-feeding/reset API is provided.
///
/// Each pending step performs one bounded action: header; one parent pair and
/// kernel; one group setup; one GroupHasher kernel (<=4096 bytes or two hashes);
/// or final EOF commit. For P visited parents and C selected fixed chunks, a valid
/// nonempty proof takes 2+P+2*C actions; empty file takes 4. Constructor/fixed-array
/// setup is bounded. These are work units, not constant wall time.
///
/// The caller checks is_finished, charges one persistent unit, then steps. Yields,
/// completed groups and subsequent output/copy must not renew its total allowance.
/// No exact-total planning scan or budget is hidden here. There are three separate
/// fixed frontiers: traversal, expected hashes, and the current group reducer.
pub struct ProofVerifier<'a> {
    proof: &'a [u8],
    root: [u8; OUTPUT_BYTES],
    size: u64,
    block: BlockSize,
    request: Range<u64>,
    traversal: RangeTraversal,
    expected: [Option<Hash>; 53],
    occupied: usize,
    cursor: usize,
    header: bool,
    empty_pending: bool,
    group: Option<Pending<'a>>,
    complete: bool,
    error: Option<ProofError>,
}

impl<'a> ProofVerifier<'a> {
    /// Admit geometry, reject empty requests for nonempty files, then check all
    /// expected-root words. Reads no proof bytes and performs no hash kernel.
    ///
    /// Geometry admits logs 0..=51 on 64-bit and 0..=19 on 32-bit, even for empty
    /// files. This concerns integer geometry, not allocation Layout or the safety
    /// of the same BlockSize in unchecked BaoTree helpers. Range requires
    /// start <= end <= size. Empty file 0..0 still requires its canonical ROOT.
    pub fn new(
        proof: &'a [u8],
        root: [u8; OUTPUT_BYTES],
        size: u64,
        block: BlockSize,
        request: Range<u64>,
    ) -> Result<Self, ProofError> {
        let traversal =
            RangeTraversal::new(size, block, request.clone()).map_err(ProofError::Geometry)?;
        if size != 0 && request.is_empty() {
            return Err(ProofError::EmptyRequest);
        }
        if let Some(word) = noncanonical(&root) {
            return Err(ProofError::NonCanonicalRoot { word });
        }
        Ok(Self {
            proof,
            root,
            size,
            block,
            request,
            traversal,
            expected: [None; 53],
            occupied: 0,
            cursor: 0,
            header: false,
            empty_pending: size == 0,
            group: None,
            complete: false,
            error: None,
        })
    }

    /// True after either exact EOF completion or a sticky processing error.
    pub fn is_finished(&self) -> bool {
        self.complete || self.error.is_some()
    }

    /// Perform one action. Pending returns None; only the separate EOF action
    /// emits Some(view). Successful terminal polls return None without work;
    /// failed terminal polls return the identical error without work.
    pub fn step(&mut self) -> Result<Option<VerifiedProof<'a>>, ProofError> {
        if let Some(error) = self.error {
            return Err(error);
        }
        if self.complete {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let result = self.advance();
        if let Err(error) = result {
            self.error = Some(error);
            self.group = None;
        }
        result
    }

    fn advance(&mut self) -> Result<Option<VerifiedProof<'a>>, ProofError> {
        if !self.header {
            let declared = u64::from_le_bytes(self.take(8)?.try_into().expect("eight-byte header"));
            if declared != self.size {
                return Err(ProofError::SizeMismatch {
                    expected: self.size,
                    declared,
                });
            }
            self.expected[0] = Some(Hash::from_bytes(self.root));
            self.occupied = 1;
            self.header = true;
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if let Some(group) = self.group.as_mut() {
            if let Some(hash) = group.reducer.step() {
                if hash != group.expected {
                    return Err(ProofError::LeafMismatch {
                        start_chunk: group.start_chunk,
                    });
                }
                self.group = None;
            }
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if self.empty_pending {
            self.empty_pending = false;
            self.start_group(0, 0, true)?;
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if let Some(item) = self.traversal.next() {
            match item {
                BaoChunk::Parent {
                    node,
                    is_root,
                    left,
                    right,
                } => {
                    let offset = self.cursor;
                    let bytes = self.take(PAIR_BYTES)?;
                    let l = wire_hash(&bytes[..OUTPUT_BYTES], offset)?;
                    let r = wire_hash(&bytes[OUTPUT_BYTES..], offset + OUTPUT_BYTES)?;
                    let expected = self.pop();
                    if BACKEND.parent_hash(&l, &r, is_root) != expected {
                        return Err(ProofError::ParentMismatch { node });
                    }
                    if right {
                        self.push(r);
                    }
                    if left {
                        self.push(l);
                    }
                }
                BaoChunk::Leaf {
                    start_chunk,
                    size,
                    is_root,
                } => self.start_group(start_chunk, size, is_root)?,
            }
            return Ok(None);
        }
        assert_eq!(self.occupied, 0, "geometry consumes every expected hash");
        if self.cursor != self.proof.len() {
            return Err(ProofError::TrailingBytes {
                offset: self.cursor,
            });
        }
        self.complete = true;
        Ok(Some(VerifiedProof::new(
            self.proof,
            self.root,
            self.size,
            self.block,
            self.request.clone(),
        )))
    }

    fn take(&mut self, needed: usize) -> Result<&'a [u8], ProofError> {
        if needed > self.proof.len() - self.cursor {
            return Err(ProofError::Truncated {
                offset: self.cursor,
                needed,
            });
        }
        let start = self.cursor;
        self.cursor += needed;
        Ok(&self.proof[start..self.cursor])
    }

    fn start_group(&mut self, start_chunk: u64, size: usize, root: bool) -> Result<(), ProofError> {
        let data = self.take(size)?;
        let expected = self.pop();
        // Checked geometry bounds final counters by 2^52-1; admitted group sizes
        // fit usize/u64. Empty file is one zero-length leaf at counter zero.
        let reducer =
            GroupHasher::new(&BACKEND, data, start_chunk, root).expect("admitted group geometry");
        self.group = Some(Pending {
            reducer,
            expected,
            start_chunk,
        });
        Ok(())
    }
    fn pop(&mut self) -> Hash {
        self.occupied -= 1;
        self.expected[self.occupied]
            .take()
            .expect("expected hash follows geometry")
    }
    fn push(&mut self, hash: Hash) {
        // At most 52 deferred right siblings plus the current expectation.
        self.expected[self.occupied] = Some(hash);
        self.occupied += 1;
    }
}

fn noncanonical(bytes: &[u8; OUTPUT_BYTES]) -> Option<u8> {
    bytes
        .chunks_exact(8)
        .position(|word| u64::from_le_bytes(word.try_into().expect("eight-byte word")) >= P)
        .map(|word| word as u8)
}
fn wire_hash(bytes: &[u8], offset: usize) -> Result<Hash, ProofError> {
    let array = bytes.try_into().expect("one complete child hash");
    if let Some(word) = noncanonical(&array) {
        return Err(ProofError::NonCanonicalHash {
            offset: offset + usize::from(word) * 8,
        });
    }
    Ok(Hash::from_bytes(array))
}

impl fmt::Debug for ProofVerifier<'_> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("ProofVerifier")
            .field("proof_bytes", &self.proof.len())
            .field("size", &self.size)
            .field("request", &self.request)
            .field("cursor", &self.cursor)
            .field("expected", &self.occupied)
            .field("group_pending", &self.group.is_some())
            .field("finished", &self.is_finished())
            .field("error", &self.error)
            .finish_non_exhaustive()
    }
}

===== 020 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/src/proof/output.rs | 5054 bytes | SHA256 b2f4ec894d5f28524ebad8e60e6129ee15f2282aeeb51c347f4e8471072989d6 =====
use core::{fmt, iter::FusedIterator, ops::Range};

use crate::{BaoChunk, BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, traversal::RangeTraversal};

/// Complete proof validation relative to the supplied root and complete size.
/// This sealed view does not establish a particle/root association. It retains
/// only immutable proof bytes and the admitted root/size/block/request tuple;
/// successful validation establishes the fixed eight-byte header for replay.
/// It can outlive its verifier, but cannot outlive or mutate the proof storage.
pub struct VerifiedProof<'a> {
    proof: &'a [u8],
    root: [u8; 32],
    size: u64,
    block: BlockSize,
    request: Range<u64>,
}
impl<'a> VerifiedProof<'a> {
    pub(super) fn new(
        proof: &'a [u8],
        root: [u8; 32],
        size: u64,
        block: BlockSize,
        request: Range<u64>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            proof,
            root,
            size,
            block,
            request,
        }
    }
    pub fn root(&self) -> [u8; 32] {
        self.root
    }
    pub fn size(&self) -> u64 {
        self.size
    }
    pub fn block_size(&self) -> BlockSize {
        self.block
    }
    /// The original half-open requested byte interval, independent of group cover.
    pub fn request(&self) -> Range<u64> {
        self.request.clone()
    }

    /// Start bounded geometry replay without scanning, rehashing or copying bytes.
    /// Each pending next is one separately chargeable parent/leaf action. A caller
    /// copies only the requested intersection of each whole leaf under its own
    /// persistent work/storage limits. An empty verified file emits no items.
    pub fn items(&self) -> ProofItems<'a> {
        ProofItems {
            proof: self.proof,
            cursor: 8,
            traversal: RangeTraversal::new(self.size, self.block, self.request.clone())
                .expect("sealed geometry"),
        }
    }
}

/// One output action. Public slice/coordinate values are not sealed authority;
/// obtain them through a completed view and its externally trusted root.
pub enum ProofItem<'a> {
    /// Opaque parent skip, exactly 64 encoded bytes. Count it positionally and
    /// charge its action; replay does not scan across it to find a payload.
    Parent,
    /// Whole selected group (only the final file group may be short).
    Leaf {
        file_offset: u64,
        proof_offset: usize,
        bytes: &'a [u8],
    },
}

/// Fixed-frontier output cursor. Every pending next returns one item; terminal
/// polls are fused, with no hashing, allocation or copying in any action.
pub struct ProofItems<'a> {
    proof: &'a [u8],
    cursor: usize,
    traversal: RangeTraversal,
}
impl ProofItems<'_> {
    /// True immediately after the final emission, or for an empty verified file.
    pub fn is_complete(&self) -> bool {
        self.traversal.is_complete()
    }
}
impl<'a> Iterator for ProofItems<'a> {
    type Item = ProofItem<'a>;
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        match self.traversal.next()? {
            BaoChunk::Parent { .. } => {
                self.cursor += super::PAIR_BYTES;
                Some(ProofItem::Parent)
            }
            BaoChunk::Leaf {
                start_chunk, size, ..
            } => {
                let proof_offset = self.cursor;
                self.cursor += size;
                // The sealed immutable proof already passed these exact extents;
                // checked traversal bounds start_chunk*4096 by the file length.
                Some(ProofItem::Leaf {
                    file_offset: start_chunk * CHUNK_SIZE as u64,
                    proof_offset,
                    bytes: &self.proof[proof_offset..self.cursor],
                })
            }
        }
    }
}
impl FusedIterator for ProofItems<'_> {}

impl fmt::Debug for VerifiedProof<'_> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("VerifiedProof")
            .field("proof_bytes", &self.proof.len())
            .field("root", &self.root)
            .field("size", &self.size)
            .field("block", &self.block)
            .field("request", &self.request)
            .finish()
    }
}
impl fmt::Debug for ProofItems<'_> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("ProofItems")
            .field("proof_bytes", &self.proof.len())
            .field("cursor", &self.cursor)
            .field("complete", &self.is_complete())
            .finish()
    }
}
impl fmt::Debug for ProofItem<'_> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        match self {
            Self::Parent => f.write_str("Parent"),
            Self::Leaf {
                file_offset,
                proof_offset,
                bytes,
            } => f
                .debug_struct("Leaf")
                .field("file_offset", file_offset)
                .field("proof_offset", proof_offset)
                .field("bytes", &bytes.len())
                .finish(),
        }
    }
}

===== 021 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/tests/proof.rs | 10191 bytes | SHA256 9c7a06da1f77d9966e940c29aee04184c958f5513224128ba19b7113b28c4eb9 =====
//! Literal schedules, borrowed output and external scheduling; no legacy roundtrip oracle.
#[path = "support/proof.rs"]
mod support;

use cyber_bao::{
    BlockSize,
    proof::{ProofItem, ProofVerifier, VerifiedProof},
};
use support::{Case, cases, named};

fn finish(c: &Case) -> VerifiedProof<'_> {
    let mut job =
        ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone()).unwrap();
    for index in 1..c.actions {
        assert!(!job.is_finished(), "{} action {index}", c.name);
        assert!(job.step().unwrap().is_none(), "{} action {index}", c.name);
    }
    assert!(!job.is_finished());
    let view = job.step().unwrap().expect("EOF action seals once");
    assert!(job.is_finished());
    for _ in 0..10 {
        assert!(job.step().unwrap().is_none());
    }
    view
}

fn output(c: &Case, view: &VerifiedProof<'_>) {
    assert_eq!(view.root(), c.root);
    assert_eq!(view.size(), c.size);
    assert_eq!(view.block_size(), BlockSize(c.log));
    assert_eq!(view.request(), c.request);
    let mut changed_copy = view.request();
    changed_copy.start = u64::MAX;
    assert_ne!(changed_copy, view.request());
    let mut items = view.items();
    let mut other = view.items();
    fn fused<T: std::iter::FusedIterator>(_: &T) {}
    fused(&items);
    assert_eq!(items.is_complete(), c.output.is_empty());
    let mut requested = Vec::new();
    for (index, expected) in c.output.iter().enumerate() {
        assert!(!items.is_complete());
        let item = items.next().unwrap();
        let parallel = other.next().unwrap();
        match (item, parallel, expected) {
            (ProofItem::Parent, ProofItem::Parent, None) => {}
            (
                ProofItem::Leaf {
                    file_offset,
                    proof_offset,
                    bytes,
                },
                ProofItem::Leaf {
                    file_offset: f,
                    proof_offset: p,
                    bytes: b,
                },
                Some((offset, file, len)),
            ) => {
                assert_eq!(
                    (proof_offset, file_offset, bytes.len()),
                    (*offset, *file, *len)
                );
                assert_eq!((f, p, b), (file_offset, proof_offset, bytes));
                assert_eq!(bytes, &c.wire[*offset..*offset + *len]);
                assert_eq!(bytes.as_ptr(), c.wire[*offset..].as_ptr());
                let start = c.request.start.max(file_offset);
                let end = c
                    .request
                    .end
                    .min(file_offset + u64::try_from(bytes.len()).unwrap());
                requested.extend_from_slice(
                    &bytes[usize::try_from(start - file_offset).unwrap()
                        ..usize::try_from(end - file_offset).unwrap()],
                );
            }
            _ => panic!("{} event {index}", c.name),
        }
        assert_eq!(items.is_complete(), index + 1 == c.output.len());
    }
    for _ in 0..10 {
        assert!(items.next().is_none());
        assert!(other.next().is_none());
    }
    if c.name == "hello" {
        assert_eq!(requested, b"ell");
    } else if c.size != 0 {
        let body = support::data(usize::try_from(c.size).unwrap());
        assert_eq!(
            requested,
            body[usize::try_from(c.request.start).unwrap()
                ..usize::try_from(c.request.end).unwrap()]
        );
    } else {
        assert!(requested.is_empty());
    }
}

#[test]
fn literal_schedules_and_exact_borrowed_coordinates() {
    for c in cases() {
        let view = finish(&c);
        output(&c, &view);
    }
}

#[test]
fn caller_quotas_and_total_are_not_renewed_by_groups_or_yields() {
    for c in cases() {
        for quota in [1, 2, c.actions, c.actions + 1] {
            for limit in [c.actions - 1, c.actions, c.actions + 1] {
                let mut job = ProofVerifier::new(
                    &c.wire,
                    c.root,
                    c.size,
                    BlockSize(c.log),
                    c.request.clone(),
                )
                .unwrap();
                let mut paid = 0;
                let mut completed = None;
                while !job.is_finished() && paid < limit {
                    for _ in 0..quota {
                        if job.is_finished() || paid == limit {
                            break;
                        }
                        paid += 1; // external harness budget, not a Radio or Kadek API
                        if let Some(view) = job.step().unwrap() {
                            completed = Some(view);
                        }
                    }
                }
                assert_eq!(paid, limit.min(c.actions));
                assert_eq!(completed.is_some(), limit >= c.actions);
                assert_eq!(job.is_finished(), limit >= c.actions);
                if let Some(view) = completed {
                    output(&c, &view);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn jobs_can_interleave_and_views_outlive_verifiers_and_cursors() {
    let a = named("three-boundary");
    let b = named("grouped-left");
    let (va, vb) = {
        let mut ja =
            ProofVerifier::new(&a.wire, a.root, a.size, BlockSize(a.log), a.request.clone())
                .unwrap();
        let mut jb =
            ProofVerifier::new(&b.wire, b.root, b.size, BlockSize(b.log), b.request.clone())
                .unwrap();
        for _ in 1..b.actions {
            assert!(ja.step().unwrap().is_none());
            assert!(jb.step().unwrap().is_none());
        }
        let vb = jb.step().unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(ja.step().unwrap().is_none());
        let va = ja.step().unwrap().unwrap();
        (va, vb)
    };
    output(&a, &va);
    output(&b, &vb);
    let kept = {
        let mut items = vb.items();
        assert!(matches!(items.next(), Some(ProofItem::Parent)));
        match items.next().unwrap() {
            ProofItem::Leaf { bytes, .. } => bytes,
            _ => unreachable!(),
        }
    };
    assert_eq!(kept, &b.wire[72..]);
}

#[test]
fn cancelling_at_each_pending_stage_publishes_nothing() {
    for c in cases() {
        for stop in 0..c.actions {
            let mut job =
                ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone())
                    .unwrap();
            for _ in 0..stop {
                assert!(job.step().unwrap().is_none());
            }
            assert!(!job.is_finished());
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn debug_does_not_print_payload() {
    let c = named("hello");
    let job =
        ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone()).unwrap();
    assert!(!format!("{job:?}").contains("104, 101, 108, 108, 111"));
    let view = finish(&c);
    assert!(!format!("{view:?}").contains("104, 101, 108, 108, 111"));
    let mut items = view.items();
    assert!(!format!("{items:?}").contains("104, 101, 108, 108, 111"));
    assert!(!format!("{:?}", items.next().unwrap()).contains("104, 101, 108, 108, 111"));
}

#[test]
fn parent_after_payload_resumes_the_frontier_and_borrowed_output() {
    // Independent four-leaf tree and literal carrier; no encoder or traversal oracle.
    let body: Vec<u8> = (0..16384)
        .map(|i| ((29 * i + 7 * (i / 4096)) % 251) as u8)
        .collect();
    let l0 = support::leaf(&body[..4096], 0);
    let l1 = support::leaf(&body[4096..8192], 1);
    let l2 = support::leaf(&body[8192..12288], 2);
    let l3 = support::leaf(&body[12288..], 3);
    let p01 = support::parent(l0, l1, false);
    let p23 = support::parent(l2, l3, false);
    let root = support::parent(p01, p23, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *root.as_bytes(),
        support::digest("e621f66b42fb61b260200dfa649d3f16d35cde5af2e8c04cc2551c109f00c6f6")
    );
    let mut wire = u64::try_from(body.len()).unwrap().to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    support::pair(&mut wire, p01, p23);
    support::pair(&mut wire, l0, l1);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[..8192]);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 8328);
    support::pair(&mut wire, l2, l3);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[8192..12288]);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 12488);
    let c = Case {
        name: "four-interleaved",
        root: *root.as_bytes(),
        size: 16384,
        log: 0,
        request: 4095..8193,
        wire,
        actions: 11,
        output: vec![
            None,
            None,
            Some((136, 0, 4096)),
            Some((4232, 4096, 4096)),
            None,
            Some((8392, 8192, 4096)),
        ],
    };
    for stop in 0..11 {
        let mut job =
            ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(0), 4095..8193).unwrap();
        for _ in 0..stop {
            assert!(job.step().unwrap().is_none());
        }
        assert!(!job.is_finished());
    }
    let view = finish(&c);
    let mut items = view.items();
    let mut selected = Vec::new();
    for expected in &c.output {
        assert!(!items.is_complete());
        match (items.next().unwrap(), expected) {
            (ProofItem::Parent, None) => {}
            (
                ProofItem::Leaf {
                    proof_offset,
                    file_offset,
                    bytes,
                },
                Some((p, f, n)),
            ) => {
                assert_eq!((proof_offset, file_offset, bytes.len()), (*p, *f, *n));
                assert_eq!(bytes.as_ptr(), c.wire[*p..].as_ptr());
                let begin = usize::try_from(*f).unwrap();
                assert_eq!(bytes, &body[begin..begin + *n]);
                let start = 4095u64.max(*f) - *f;
                let end = 8193u64.min(*f + 4096) - *f;
                selected.extend_from_slice(
                    &bytes[usize::try_from(start).unwrap()..usize::try_from(end).unwrap()],
                );
            }
            _ => panic!("literal interleaved event mismatch"),
        }
    }
    assert!(items.is_complete());
    assert!(items.next().is_none());
    assert_eq!(selected, body[4095..8193]);
    assert_ne!(&c.wire[4231..4231 + selected.len()], selected);
}

===== 022 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/tests/proof_failures.rs | 11662 bytes | SHA256 9a89df71bbdd17dec76b9907dd026a3b4104cfe77d12367b12a86140f67e1af7 =====
//! Error variants, literal coordinates and first-error precedence.
#[path = "support/proof.rs"]
mod support;

use cyber_bao::{
    BlockSize, TreeNode,
    proof::{ProofError, ProofVerifier},
    traversal::RangeTraversalError,
};
use hemera::{Hash, field::P};
use support::{Case, digest, named};

fn failure(c: &Case, expected: ProofError, action: usize) {
    let mut job =
        ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone()).unwrap();
    for i in 1..action {
        assert!(
            job.step().unwrap().is_none(),
            "{} prematurely completed at {i}",
            c.name
        );
        assert!(!job.is_finished());
    }
    assert_eq!(
        job.step().unwrap_err(),
        expected,
        "{} action {action}",
        c.name
    );
    assert!(job.is_finished());
    for _ in 0..10 {
        assert_eq!(job.step().unwrap_err(), expected);
    }
}

#[test]
#[allow(clippy::reversed_empty_ranges)] // Intentionally invalid request geometry.
fn geometry_precedes_empty_request_and_root_canonicality() {
    for log in 0..=255 {
        let admitted = match usize::BITS {
            64 => log <= 51,
            32 => log <= 19,
            _ => panic!("unsupported host width"),
        };
        for size in [0, 1, u64::MAX] {
            let request = if size == 0 { 0..0 } else { 0..1 };
            let result = ProofVerifier::new(&[], [0; 32], size, BlockSize(log), request);
            if admitted {
                assert!(result.is_ok());
            } else {
                assert_eq!(
                    result.unwrap_err(),
                    ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidBlockSize)
                );
            }
        }
    }
    for request in [2..1, 0..6] {
        assert_eq!(
            ProofVerifier::new(&[], [255; 32], 5, BlockSize(0), request).unwrap_err(),
            ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidRange)
        );
    }
    assert_eq!(
        ProofVerifier::new(&[], [255; 32], 0, BlockSize(255), 1..0).unwrap_err(),
        ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidBlockSize)
    );
    assert_eq!(
        ProofVerifier::new(&[], [255; 32], 0, BlockSize(0), 0..1).unwrap_err(),
        ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidRange)
    );
    for at in [0, 2, 5] {
        assert_eq!(
            ProofVerifier::new(&[], [255; 32], 5, BlockSize(0), at..at).unwrap_err(),
            ProofError::EmptyRequest
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn each_expected_root_word_has_exact_canonical_boundary() {
    for word in 0..4 {
        for value in [0, P - 1, P, u64::MAX] {
            let mut root = [0; 32];
            root[word * 8..word * 8 + 8].copy_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
            let result = ProofVerifier::new(&[], root, 1, BlockSize(0), 0..1);
            if value < P {
                assert!(result.is_ok());
            } else {
                assert_eq!(
                    result.unwrap_err(),
                    ProofError::NonCanonicalRoot {
                        word: u8::try_from(word).unwrap()
                    }
                );
            }
        }
    }
    assert_eq!(
        ProofVerifier::new(&[], [255; 32], 1, BlockSize(0), 0..1).unwrap_err(),
        ProofError::NonCanonicalRoot { word: 0 }
    );
}

#[test]
fn header_size_is_trusted_input_and_every_truncation_is_exact() {
    for declared in [0, 4, 6, 4096, u64::MAX] {
        let mut c = named("hello");
        c.wire[..8].copy_from_slice(&declared.to_le_bytes());
        failure(
            &c,
            ProofError::SizeMismatch {
                expected: 5,
                declared,
            },
            1,
        );
    }
    for name in ["three-interior", "grouped-left"] {
        let mut c = named(name);
        let full = c.wire.clone();
        for len in 0..full.len() {
            c.wire = full[..len].to_vec();
            let (offset, needed, action) = if len < 8 {
                (0, 8, 1)
            } else if len < 72 {
                (8, 64, 2)
            } else if name == "three-interior" && len < 136 {
                (72, 64, 3)
            } else if name == "three-interior" {
                (136, 4096, 4)
            } else {
                (72, 8192, 3)
            };
            failure(&c, ProofError::Truncated { offset, needed }, action);
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn both_children_all_words_reject_noncanonical_before_hashing() {
    for pair_start in [8, 72] {
        for child in [0, 32] {
            for word in 0..4 {
                for value in [P, u64::MAX] {
                    let mut c = named("three-interior");
                    let offset = pair_start + child + word * 8;
                    c.wire[offset..offset + 8].copy_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
                    failure(
                        &c,
                        ProofError::NonCanonicalHash { offset },
                        if pair_start == 8 { 2 } else { 3 },
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }
    let mut c = named("three-interior");
    c.wire[8..16].copy_from_slice(&P.to_le_bytes());
    c.wire.truncate(71);
    failure(
        &c,
        ProofError::Truncated {
            offset: 8,
            needed: 64,
        },
        2,
    );
}

#[test]
fn canonical_parent_and_payload_corruption_never_publish() {
    for pair_start in [8, 72] {
        let mut c = named("three-interior");
        let left = c.wire[pair_start..pair_start + 32].to_vec();
        c.wire
            .copy_within(pair_start + 32..pair_start + 64, pair_start);
        c.wire[pair_start + 32..pair_start + 64].copy_from_slice(&left);
        failure(
            &c,
            ProofError::ParentMismatch {
                node: TreeNode::new(if pair_start == 8 { 3 } else { 1 }),
            },
            if pair_start == 8 { 2 } else { 3 },
        );
    }
    let mut c = named("three-interior");
    c.root = [0; 32];
    failure(
        &c,
        ProofError::ParentMismatch {
            node: TreeNode::new(3),
        },
        2,
    );
    let mut c = named("three-interior");
    c.wire[40..72].fill(0);
    failure(
        &c,
        ProofError::ParentMismatch {
            node: TreeNode::new(3),
        },
        2,
    );
    for (offset, start_chunk, action) in [(136, 0, 5), (4232, 1, 7)] {
        let mut c = named("three-boundary");
        c.wire[offset] ^= 1;
        failure(&c, ProofError::LeafMismatch { start_chunk }, action);
    }
    let mut c = named("sole-group");
    c.root = digest("11890a7e7cf1e4de463a04bf53bfdf269d627983a221daaf9029a3ff43297389");
    failure(&c, ProofError::LeafMismatch { start_chunk: 0 }, 5);
}

#[test]
fn representation_aliases_are_not_normalized() {
    let d = support::data(4096);
    let left = support::leaf(&d, 0);
    let zero = Hash::from_bytes([0; 32]);
    let root = support::parent(left, zero, true);
    let mut c = named("three-right");
    c.root = *root.as_bytes();
    c.size = 8192;
    c.request = 0..1;
    c.wire = 8192u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    support::pair(&mut c.wire, left, zero);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d);
    for word in 0..4 {
        let mut alias = c.wire.clone();
        let offset = 40 + word * 8;
        alias[offset..offset + 8].copy_from_slice(&P.to_le_bytes());
        std::mem::swap(&mut c.wire, &mut alias);
        failure(&c, ProofError::NonCanonicalHash { offset }, 2);
        std::mem::swap(&mut c.wire, &mut alias);
    }
}

#[test]
fn absolute_chunk_counter_is_not_relative_to_the_selected_group() {
    let mut c = named("three-right");
    let payload = c.wire[72..].to_vec();
    let wrong = support::leaf(&payload, 0);
    let zero = Hash::from_bytes([0; 32]);
    c.root = *support::parent(zero, wrong, true).as_bytes();
    c.wire = 12288u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    support::pair(&mut c.wire, zero, wrong);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&payload);
    failure(&c, ProofError::LeafMismatch { start_chunk: 2 }, 4);
}

#[test]
fn exact_eof_is_a_separate_required_commit() {
    for name in ["empty", "hello", "three-boundary", "sole-group"] {
        for concatenate in [false, true] {
            let mut c = named(name);
            let end = c.wire.len();
            if concatenate {
                c.wire.extend_from_slice(&c.wire.clone());
            } else {
                c.wire.push(0);
            }
            failure(&c, ProofError::TrailingBytes { offset: end }, c.actions);
        }
    }
    let mut c = named("empty");
    c.root = [0; 32];
    failure(&c, ProofError::LeafMismatch { start_chunk: 0 }, 3);
    for name in ["hello", "three-right"] {
        let mut c = named(name);
        c.wire.truncate(8);
        failure(
            &c,
            ProofError::Truncated {
                offset: 8,
                needed: if name == "hello" { 5 } else { 64 },
            },
            2,
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn maximum_frontier_prefix_uses_literal_small_missing_leaf() {
    let (root, wire) = support::deep_prefix();
    let c = Case {
        name: "deep",
        root,
        wire,
        size: u64::MAX,
        log: 0,
        request: 0..u64::MAX,
        actions: 56,
        output: Vec::new(),
    };
    failure(
        &c,
        ProofError::Truncated {
            offset: 7432,
            needed: 4096,
        },
        56,
    );
}

#[test]
#[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
fn largest_group_width_is_geometry_not_allocation_admission() {
    let zero = Hash::from_bytes([0; 32]);
    let root = support::parent(zero, zero, true);
    let mut wire = u64::MAX.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    support::pair(&mut wire, zero, zero);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 72);
    let c = Case {
        name: "large",
        root: *root.as_bytes(),
        wire,
        size: u64::MAX,
        log: 51,
        request: 0..1,
        actions: 3,
        output: Vec::new(),
    };
    failure(
        &c,
        ProofError::Truncated {
            offset: 72,
            needed: 1usize << 63,
        },
        3,
    );
}

#[test]
fn diagnostic_strings_and_core_error_trait_are_frozen() {
    fn core_error<E: core::error::Error>() {}
    core_error::<ProofError>();
    for (error, text) in [
        (
            ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidBlockSize),
            "invalid traversal block size",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::Geometry(RangeTraversalError::InvalidRange),
            "invalid traversal byte range",
        ),
        (ProofError::EmptyRequest, "empty request for nonempty file"),
        (
            ProofError::NonCanonicalRoot { word: 2 },
            "noncanonical expected root word 2",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::Truncated {
                offset: 72,
                needed: 4096,
            },
            "truncated proof at 72: need 4096 bytes",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::SizeMismatch {
                expected: 5,
                declared: 6,
            },
            "proof size 6 differs from expected 5",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::NonCanonicalHash { offset: 40 },
            "noncanonical proof hash word at 40",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::ParentMismatch {
                node: TreeNode::new(3),
            },
            "proof parent mismatch at node 3",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::LeafMismatch { start_chunk: 2 },
            "proof leaf mismatch at chunk 2",
        ),
        (
            ProofError::TrailingBytes { offset: 13 },
            "trailing proof bytes at 13",
        ),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(error.to_string(), text);
    }
}

===== 023 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/tests/proof_allocation.rs | 4240 bytes | SHA256 b8dc7985cc0cf68e68530d19cc8040b8ed28773c67965b5170ad571645b45df5 =====
//! Concrete host observation; fixture allocation and destruction are outside it.
#[path = "support/proof.rs"]
mod support;

use std::{
    alloc::{GlobalAlloc, Layout, System},
    cell::Cell,
    hint::black_box,
};

use cyber_bao::{BlockSize, proof::ProofVerifier};

thread_local! {
    static ACTIVE: Cell<bool> = const { Cell::new(false) };
    static COUNT: Cell<usize> = const { Cell::new(0) };
}
struct Allocator;
fn record() {
    ACTIVE.with(|active| {
        if active.get() {
            COUNT.with(|n| n.set(n.get() + 1));
        }
    });
}
unsafe impl GlobalAlloc for Allocator {
    unsafe fn alloc(&self, layout: Layout) -> *mut u8 {
        record();
        // SAFETY: preserve the allocator's incoming layout.
        unsafe { System.alloc(layout) }
    }
    unsafe fn alloc_zeroed(&self, layout: Layout) -> *mut u8 {
        record();
        // SAFETY: preserve the allocator's incoming layout.
        unsafe { System.alloc_zeroed(layout) }
    }
    unsafe fn realloc(&self, ptr: *mut u8, layout: Layout, size: usize) -> *mut u8 {
        record();
        // SAFETY: preserve the original pointer/layout and requested new size.
        unsafe { System.realloc(ptr, layout, size) }
    }
    unsafe fn dealloc(&self, ptr: *mut u8, layout: Layout) {
        // SAFETY: preserve the original allocation identity; frees are not allocs.
        unsafe { System.dealloc(ptr, layout) }
    }
}
#[global_allocator]
static ALLOCATOR: Allocator = Allocator;

fn observed<T>(f: impl FnOnce() -> T) -> T {
    struct Guard;
    impl Drop for Guard {
        fn drop(&mut self) {
            ACTIVE.with(|a| a.set(false));
        }
    }
    COUNT.with(|n| n.set(0));
    ACTIVE.with(|a| assert!(!a.replace(true)));
    let guard = Guard;
    let result = f();
    drop(guard);
    assert_eq!(COUNT.with(Cell::get), 0);
    result
}

#[test]
fn concrete_verifier_all_stages_output_failure_and_cancel_allocate_nothing() {
    COUNT.with(|n| n.set(0));
    ACTIVE.with(|a| a.set(true));
    let positive = black_box(vec![17u8; 17]);
    ACTIVE.with(|a| a.set(false));
    assert!(COUNT.with(Cell::get) > 0);
    drop(positive);
    let all = support::cases(); // construction and eventual drop outside observation
    for c in &all {
        let mut job = observed(|| {
            ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone())
                .unwrap()
        });
        let mut view = None;
        while !job.is_finished() {
            if let Some(v) = observed(|| job.step().unwrap()) {
                view = Some(v);
            }
        }
        let view = view.unwrap();
        for _ in 0..10 {
            assert!(observed(|| job.step().unwrap()).is_none());
        }
        let mut items = observed(|| view.items());
        while !items.is_complete() {
            black_box(observed(|| items.next()));
        }
        for _ in 0..10 {
            assert!(observed(|| items.next()).is_none());
        }
        observed(|| {
            black_box(items);
            black_box(view);
            black_box(job);
        });
        for stop in 0..c.actions {
            let mut job = observed(|| {
                ProofVerifier::new(&c.wire, c.root, c.size, BlockSize(c.log), c.request.clone())
                    .unwrap()
            });
            for _ in 0..stop {
                assert!(observed(|| job.step().unwrap()).is_none());
            }
            observed(|| {
                black_box(job);
            });
        }
    }
    let mut corrupt = support::named("three-boundary");
    corrupt.wire.push(0);
    let mut job = observed(|| {
        ProofVerifier::new(
            &corrupt.wire,
            corrupt.root,
            corrupt.size,
            BlockSize(corrupt.log),
            corrupt.request.clone(),
        )
        .unwrap()
    });
    for _ in 1..corrupt.actions {
        assert!(observed(|| job.step().unwrap()).is_none());
    }
    let error = observed(|| job.step().unwrap_err());
    for _ in 0..10 {
        assert_eq!(observed(|| job.step().unwrap_err()), error);
    }
    observed(|| {
        black_box(job);
    });
    assert!(observed(|| ProofVerifier::new(&[], [0; 32], 0, BlockSize(255), 0..0)).is_err());
}

===== 024 BEGIN Candidate full cyber-bao/tests/support/proof.rs | 7549 bytes | SHA256 a28d04cb2d1cee78f950b895956830fe849cfe97256649b3a2088200994c5ddd =====
#![allow(dead_code)]
//! Independent small-tree expressions and literal carrier coordinates.
use std::ops::Range;

use cyber_bao::{HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend};
use hemera::Hash;

pub struct Case {
    pub name: &'static str,
    pub root: [u8; 32],
    pub size: u64,
    pub log: u8,
    pub request: Range<u64>,
    pub wire: Vec<u8>,
    pub actions: usize,
    /// None is one opaque parent; Some is (proof offset, file offset, length).
    pub output: Vec<Option<(usize, u64, usize)>>,
}

pub fn data(n: usize) -> Vec<u8> {
    (0..n).map(|i| ((31 * i + i / 4096) % 256) as u8).collect()
}
pub fn digest(hex: &str) -> [u8; 32] {
    std::array::from_fn(|i| u8::from_str_radix(&hex[2 * i..2 * i + 2], 16).unwrap())
}
pub fn leaf(data: &[u8], counter: u64) -> Hash {
    Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(data, counter, false)
}
pub fn parent(left: Hash, right: Hash, root: bool) -> Hash {
    Poseidon2Backend.parent_hash(&left, &right, root)
}
pub fn pair(wire: &mut Vec<u8>, left: Hash, right: Hash) {
    wire.extend_from_slice(left.as_bytes());
    wire.extend_from_slice(right.as_bytes());
}
fn case(name: &'static str, root: Hash, size: u64, log: u8, request: Range<u64>) -> Case {
    Case {
        name,
        root: *root.as_bytes(),
        size,
        log,
        request,
        wire: size.to_le_bytes().to_vec(),
        actions: 0,
        output: Vec::new(),
    }
}
pub fn cases() -> Vec<Case> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    let empty = Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(&[], 0, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *empty.as_bytes(),
        digest("ea57b2e6b1ec7d2de11b15cb6d7060dd61d247fe0fbf5f7d3fb97a7be9328552")
    );
    let mut c = case("empty", empty, 0, 0, 0..0);
    c.actions = 4;
    out.push(c);
    let hello = Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(b"hello", 0, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *hello.as_bytes(),
        digest("626fa46e4e7bd5c87d630eef8333931a0b9198587400a0191eae7821692880d7")
    );
    let mut c = case("hello", hello, 5, 0, 1..4);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(b"hello");
    c.actions = 4;
    c.output = vec![Some((8, 0, 5))];
    out.push(c);

    let d = data(12288);
    let l0 = leaf(&d[..4096], 0);
    let l1 = leaf(&d[4096..8192], 1);
    let l2 = leaf(&d[8192..], 2);
    let p01 = parent(l0, l1, false);
    let root = parent(p01, l2, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *root.as_bytes(),
        digest("373d0ded6b2bd351ba575cdc690ca79bbc04af1a49844f67140ce7a10e672992")
    );
    let mut c = case("three-right", root, 12288, 0, 8192..8193);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[8192..]);
    c.actions = 5;
    c.output = vec![None, Some((72, 8192, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("three-interior", root, 12288, 0, 4097..4100);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
    pair(&mut c.wire, l0, l1);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[4096..8192]);
    c.actions = 6;
    c.output = vec![None, None, Some((136, 4096, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("three-boundary", root, 12288, 0, 4095..4097);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
    pair(&mut c.wire, l0, l1);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[..8192]);
    c.actions = 8;
    c.output = vec![None, None, Some((136, 0, 4096)), Some((4232, 4096, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("grouped-left", root, 12288, 1, 4095..4097);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[..8192]);
    c.actions = 7;
    c.output = vec![None, Some((72, 0, 8192))];
    out.push(c);
    let short = data(8193);
    let final_cv = leaf(&short[8192..], 2);
    let short_root = parent(p01, final_cv, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *short_root.as_bytes(),
        digest("40c6bd68b6e89443753bce2dd95fd159da63a7399340fa6070a762d102d9e3ae")
    );
    let mut c = case("final-short", short_root, 8193, 1, 8192..8193);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, final_cv);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&short[8192..]);
    c.actions = 5;
    c.output = vec![None, Some((72, 8192, 1))];
    out.push(c);
    let root2 = parent(l0, l1, true);
    assert_eq!(
        *root2.as_bytes(),
        digest("2abf65dd26465a7352707d79d18ff3aab9ee5d97614a774619c79ed527e20190")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        *p01.as_bytes(),
        digest("11890a7e7cf1e4de463a04bf53bfdf269d627983a221daaf9029a3ff43297389")
    );
    assert_ne!(root2, p01);
    let mut c = case("sole-group", root2, 8192, 1, 1..8191);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[..8192]);
    c.actions = 6;
    c.output = vec![Some((8, 0, 8192))];
    out.push(c);

    for (log, actions) in [(0, 10), (1, 9), (2, 8), (4, 8)] {
        let mut c = case("full-three", root, 12288, log, 0..12288);
        if log == 0 {
            pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
            pair(&mut c.wire, l0, l1);
            c.output = vec![
                None,
                None,
                Some((136, 0, 4096)),
                Some((4232, 4096, 4096)),
                Some((8328, 8192, 4096)),
            ];
        } else if log == 1 {
            pair(&mut c.wire, p01, l2);
            c.output = vec![None, Some((72, 0, 8192)), Some((8264, 8192, 4096))];
        } else {
            c.output = vec![Some((8, 0, 12288))];
        }
        c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d);
        c.actions = actions;
        out.push(c);
    }
    let d = data(7 * 4096);
    let l: Vec<_> = d
        .chunks(4096)
        .enumerate()
        .map(|(i, b)| leaf(b, u64::try_from(i).unwrap()))
        .collect();
    let p01 = parent(l[0], l[1], false);
    let p23 = parent(l[2], l[3], false);
    let p03 = parent(p01, p23, false);
    let p45 = parent(l[4], l[5], false);
    let p46 = parent(p45, l[6], false);
    let root5 = parent(p03, l[4], true);
    let root7 = parent(p03, p46, true);
    let mut c = case("five-right", root5, 5 * 4096, 0, 16384..16385);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p03, l[4]);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[16384..20480]);
    c.actions = 5;
    c.output = vec![None, Some((72, 16384, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("five-interior", root5, 5 * 4096, 0, 12289..12290);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p03, l[4]);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p01, p23);
    pair(&mut c.wire, l[2], l[3]);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[12288..16384]);
    c.actions = 7;
    c.output = vec![None, None, None, Some((200, 12288, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("seven-right", root7, 7 * 4096, 0, 24576..24577);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p03, p46);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p45, l[6]);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[24576..]);
    c.actions = 6;
    c.output = vec![None, None, Some((136, 24576, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    let mut c = case("seven-interior", root7, 7 * 4096, 0, 20481..20482);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p03, p46);
    pair(&mut c.wire, p45, l[6]);
    pair(&mut c.wire, l[4], l[5]);
    c.wire.extend_from_slice(&d[20480..24576]);
    c.actions = 7;
    c.output = vec![None, None, None, Some((200, 20480, 4096))];
    out.push(c);
    out
}

pub fn named(name: &str) -> Case {
    cases().into_iter().find(|c| c.name == name).unwrap()
}

/// Synthetic commitment prefix; no actual enormous complete file is witnessed.
pub fn deep_prefix() -> ([u8; 32], Vec<u8>) {
    let d = data(4096);
    let mut left = leaf(&d, 0);
    let zero = Hash::from_bytes([0; 32]);
    let mut pairs = Vec::new();
    for level in 0..52 {
        pairs.push((left, zero));
        left = parent(left, zero, level == 51);
    }
    let mut wire = u64::MAX.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    for (left, right) in pairs.into_iter().rev() {
        pair(&mut wire, left, right);
    }
    wire.extend_from_slice(&d);
    assert_eq!(wire.len(), 7432);
    (*left.as_bytes(), wire)
}
