===== 043 BEGIN task/plan.md | 14878 bytes | SHA256 6a104b42977d594a0ac1e05cd999ec750c896f4d4e24865a0eb1718cfba34350 =====
# Bounded complete-buffer BAO proof verification

Proposed PLAN; no production implementation or approval. Serves Kadek foundation
row 4 through Radio, declared part of soft3; the future Kadek adapter reaches Cyb.
Radio implementation parent is b933829cbe273d2f31fe75ca83125f3ddd8917a2 (PR39);
research probes remain attributed to a96d86e28d384cdf656aadc4282c78c0c170c102.
Hemera is d5f0a09539360934fb32db9b2e15a91995607db6. The prerequisite RangeTraversal
is merged; an ordinary fast-forward and exact source/lock/consumer bridge preserve
the research identities. Its source equals the previously frozen candidate. Kadek's
sealed BaoAnchor is now committed at 4d9ca9ab3498238b9c1e03f6d6f160e1ec729912.

## Result and boundary

Add one concrete no_std Poseidon2 proof reader borrowing an immutable complete
proof buffer. It verifies one nonempty half-open byte request, or the empty file,
against an externally trusted complete-root/length pair. It returns a private-field
borrowed view only after every selected group, every required parent and exact
encoded EOF have passed. The view can replay one geometry item per output action
without allocating spans or copying payload. Legacy std readers/producers remain
unchanged. This is an additive minor API, with ring 1 reach, not a patch/ring-0-only
claim. The canonical-wire requirement is an intentional new-profile tightening.

Radio does not derive particle authority. The future Kadek adapter must use the
committed BaoAnchor's root, length and HemeraFixed4096V1 profile, retain that sealed
association, charge persistent work, own bounded proof/output storage and publish
only after full verification. A caller-created root or Radio view cannot create a
Kadek VerifiedRange. No Kadek adapter, network fetch, incremental byte feeding,
serialized anchor, first-partial authentication, multi-range set or legacy-reader
migration belongs to this unit.

## Carrier and admission

The borrowed carrier is exactly: eight-byte LE complete u64 length, followed by
the selected preorder parent pairs and whole selected group payloads. Both 32-byte
children are present for every visited parent, even when only one child is selected.
Request and BlockSize are separate trusted operation inputs, not wire fields.
The supplied length must equal the header. A file-backed slice's in-memory length
does not substitute for this complete file length. No suffix or concatenated proof
is permitted. A final short group consumes exactly its remaining file bytes.

ROOT, absolute 4096-byte counters, complete-left shape and concrete kernels follow
Hemera d5's tree/capacity/encoding contracts and parameters, already used by Radio
a96's GroupHasher. Grouping changes the carrier, never the canonical complete root.
Historical grouped internal CVs without ROOT are not accepted as canonical roots.
This does not equate the tree root with the separately pinned plain particle hash.

Constructor order is deterministic: RangeTraversal geometry admission (BlockSize
before range), reject an empty request when length > 0, then check the four expected
root words in ascending order. The constructor reads no proof bytes and hashes none.
Each hash word is one LE u64 and must be strictly below exported `hemera::field::P`;
check all four words in the supplied root and both children of every wire pair.
Use public Hemera constants for widths. `Hash::from_bytes` itself is a raw wrapper;
the proof reader must perform the canonical check before parent arithmetic.

Block log admits exactly the RangeTraversal range: 0..=51 on 64-bit, 0..=19 on
32-bit. This is integer geometry, not an allocation/Layout promise. Range requires
start <= end <= length. Empty file only admits 0..0: header must be zero, one empty
ROOT leaf is hashed, and EOF must be byte 8. It yields a verified empty view with
no output items. A nonempty file's empty request is rejected, including at EOF;
there is no successful mode with an unchecked root.

## Small public surface and lifetime

Names below are proposed, to be frozen by review:

```rust
ProofVerifier::new(proof: &[u8], root: [u8; 32], size: u64,
                   block: BlockSize, request: Range<u64>) -> Result<Self, ProofError>
ProofVerifier::is_finished(&self) -> bool
ProofVerifier::step(&mut self) -> Result<Option<VerifiedProof<'a>>, ProofError>
VerifiedProof::{root, size, block_size, request} // exact admitted operation inputs
VerifiedProof::items(&self) -> ProofItems<'a>
ProofItems::is_complete(&self) -> bool
impl Iterator<Item = ProofItem<'a>> + FusedIterator for ProofItems<'a>
enum ProofItem<'a> {
    Parent,
    Leaf { file_offset: u64, proof_offset: usize, bytes: &'a [u8] },
}
```

The implementation supplies explicit named lifetimes; this sketch abbreviates them.
VerifiedProof has private fields and no public constructor, conversion from raw
bytes, Deserialize, attestation or mutable-byte escape. Its accessors return the
root/size/block by value, and a cloned range. It borrows only the original immutable
proof, not the verifier. It can outlive a dropped verifier but not the proof bytes.
No allocation owner/clone is acquired. Debug prints coordinates/state, not payload.
Public ProofItem values carry slices/coordinates, not independent sealed authority;
consumers obtain trusted items through a successfully completed view and its root.

Pending successful steps return None with is_finished=false. Exact EOF completion
returns Some(view) once and sets is_finished=true. Later successful polls return
None without work. Any processing error becomes sticky and sets is_finished=true;
later polls return that same Copy error without work. Dropping cancels a pending job.
No push/flush/reset/request reuse or hidden quota renewal exists.

## Bounded machine and work

Use one RangeTraversal, one fixed 53-slot expected-hash stack, and at most one
GroupHasher borrowing the current proof group and a static Poseidon2Backend. The
expected stack follows the same frontier bound as traversal: at most 52 deferred
siblings plus one current value. Parent processing pops exactly one expectation,
hashes the pair using that node's ROOT bit, compares it, then pushes only selected
right/left expectations in that order. It does not traverse or authenticate the
unselected sibling payload. Never clone an arbitrary generic backend hash.

After constructor setup, each pending call admits exactly one of these actions:

| Action | Exact bounded work and transition |
| --- | --- |
| Header | Read at most 8 bytes, compare declared/trusted size, seed expected root. |
| Parent | Obtain one traversal item, consume exactly 64 bytes, validate left words then right words, one parent kernel, compare, push at most two expectations. |
| Group setup | Obtain one leaf item, take exactly its checked payload slice, pop its expectation, initialize the fixed reducer; no kernel. |
| Group kernel | Exactly one GroupHasher step: one <=4096-byte leaf or one two-child parent. On completing hash, compare the pending expected hash in this same action. |
| Commit | Geometry and expected stack must be empty and no group pending; check cursor == proof.len, emit the view. |

The empty file substitutes one synthetic zero-length ROOT group setup for geometry,
then one kernel. No generic reducer, traversal scan, proof-sized buffer initialization
or payload copy may be hidden in any action or constructor. Fixed 53-slot setup and
small scalar checks are bounded setup. GroupHasher admission cannot fail after
checked traversal: real group bytes fit usize/u64 and final absolute chunk counters
are <=2^52-1; use an internal invariant assertion rather than inventing a reachable
new external failure class. Stack/geometry invariants likewise follow private state.

For a valid nonempty proof let P be visited parents, L selected groups, and C the
number of 4096-byte chunks across those groups. Total actions are
`2 + P + L + sum(2*N_group-1) = 2 + P + 2*C`. Empty-file total is 4. This is an
exact oracle, not an uncharged constructor traversal. Across a u64-sized file with
N=ceil(size/4096), P <= groups-1 <= N-1, so total <=3*N+1 <2^54. Counts cannot
overflow u64. Error attempts cost their one admitted action; terminal polls cost 0.
Radio owns no budget type. The caller checks is_finished, admits one persistent
unit, then steps; it may abort on total exhaustion with no published result. A
future Kadek adapter must not reset totals on yields, completed groups or the
transition from verification to output/copy. No exact-total preflight is promised
by this Radio API; a separate charged planning walk would be an adapter choice.

Read extents use `needed <= proof.len()-cursor` before slicing/advancing. Leaf
coordinates come from the checked traversal; multiply its absolute chunk start
by 4096 only under that bound. Keep proof usize offsets distinct from file u64
offsets. No unchecked BaoTree byte helpers are used for large geometry.

## Errors and output actions

ProofError derives Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq and Eq, and implements Display
and core::error::Error. Its exact variants and precedence are:

| Location | Error and coordinate |
| --- | --- |
| Constructor | Geometry(RangeTraversalError), then EmptyRequest, then NonCanonicalRoot { word: u8 }. |
| Header | Truncated { offset: 0, needed: 8 }, then SizeMismatch { expected, declared }. |
| Parent | Truncated { offset: cursor, needed: 64 }, then NonCanonicalHash { offset: offending LE word's absolute proof offset } scanning left then right, then ParentMismatch { node: TreeNode }. |
| Group setup | Truncated { offset: cursor, needed: exact group size }. |
| Completing group | LeafMismatch { start_chunk: u64 }; zero for empty file. |
| Commit | TrailingBytes { offset: consumed proof length }. |

No EOF publication on a matching last group alone. An invalid suffix makes the
whole proof fail. No partially verified view is accessible on any failure.
Freeze exact Display messages and variant/coordinate assertions with tests before
implementation; do not make diagnostic strings the only oracle.

`items()` creates a fresh checked geometry cursor in bounded fixed setup and starts
its proof cursor at 8; construction scans/hashes/copies no proof. Each pending next
obtains exactly one geometry item. Parent advances 64 bytes and returns Parent;
leaf advances its exact size and returns that immutable slice plus file/proof
coordinates. It must not loop across parents to find a leaf. Verified immutable
input permits infallible replay; assert private invariants rather than rehash.
P+L output actions, zero for empty file. The cursor is immediately complete after
the final item and all later polls return None without work. Caller charges each
pending output action separately, then charges any byte copying separately; no
free collect/copy helper. Whole groups may extend outside the request. Copy only
their intersection with the view's exact requested range for a requested-range
packet; do not confuse wire offsets with authenticated file offsets.

## Steps, files, and gates

1. Preserve research probes and fixtures and the completed actual traversal bridge.
   → verify: exact Git identities, retained a96d measurements, identical prerequisite
   candidate/merged source, unchanged lock and owning consumers except module wiring.
2. Freeze manual fixtures and tests from finite-cases.md, then demonstrate API
   absence on the exact parent. → verify: expected missing-module compiler class,
   separated from the measured legacy behavior; no false new runtime baseline.
3. Add only concrete proof module, output view and re-export/module wiring. → verify:
   all literal cases, exact costs/coordinates, step pauses/failures, no early result.
4. Exercise lifetime/field seals, allocation and feature/build contracts. → verify:
   positive control compiler case, expected compile-error classes, host allocation
   observer, debug/release, actual no-default count, no_std cross check; no final-link
   allocator-free claim based only on a library compile.
5. Freeze gate receipts, source delta and bounded fresh CODE context. → verify:
   independent exact-parent/raw/source replay, different-vendor CODE approval before
   root's commit/merge. No production CODE review is claimed by this PLAN.

Expected production paths: cyber-bao/src/lib.rs, new cyber-bao/src/proof.rs and
cyber-bao/src/proof/output.rs. Tests: new cyber-bao/tests/proof.rs,

Task and audit paths are owned here; other source paths require a justified scope
amendment. No Kadek source or shared fixture ledger changes in this unit.

Required owner gates: strict warning-free cyber-bao debug/release tests for default,

cross check and measured MSRV. Semver against v0.1.0 plus actual-parent comparison
if needed; additive API requires minor acceptance regardless of no breaking findings.
Radio CI argv/config are frozen as support: workspace all/default/none test, Clippy,
docs, MSRV, formatting, deny and applicable native consumer gates are retained as
required gate rows, not silently replaced by the small owner suite. External/rung
closure and unavailable platform/toolchain rows must be explicit. Current parent
failure comparisons use exact failing cases/commands and identical env/profile,
never stale ancestor reds or an unnecessary second whole matrix. Local --locked,
offline, jobs=2, incremental=0 and bounded-time configuration are labeled local
strengthening, with raw command/timeout receipts. CI profile termination must be
read from actual config, not inferred. Research runs no broad CI matrix.

## Five likely failures

1. A trusted size/root becomes an arbitrary authority escape: keep Radio result
   conditional on supplied root, borrow original bytes, publish only at strict EOF;
   Kadek integration is separately sealed and outside this change.
2. ROOT or absolute counters change with grouping/selection: explicit three-leaf,
   sole-group, right-only and final-short manual carriers and historical-CV rejection.
3. Unbounded work hides in a traversal/leaf/output call: exact action schedules,
   largest-depth finite prefixes, one-item output events and source invariant review.
4. Canonical validation normalizes/reduces instead of rejecting: four independent
   word positions, both child sides and root, p-1/p/MAX boundaries before hashing.
5. Completion, failure or borrowed retention leaks partial authority: late suffix
   and last-leaf failures, sticky terminal polls, interleaved jobs, output exact ranges,
   successful/failed/cancelled drop and compile-time lifetime/field seals.

# Finite proof-verifier acceptance table

Proposed tests, frozen before the new API exists. Research fixtures are under
audit/2026-10-12-bao-proof-research/fixtures. Rust primary-probe builds the wire
manually, uses explicit chunk/parent expressions, then checks legacy producer and
reader agreement as supplemental evidence. It does not implement the proposed
reader. Its printed expected_actions are PLAN literals, not measured new-reader work.

For D[n], byte i = (31*i + floor(i/4096)) mod 256. Let Li be the non-ROOT hash of
the actual chunk at absolute counter i; P01=parent(L0,L1,false). Three-chunk ROOT
is parent(P01,L2,true). No recursive test helper infers these small tree shapes.
Future five/seven-group cases spell their parent expressions independently:
R5=P(P(P(L0,L1),P(L2,L3)),L4,ROOT);
R7=P(P(P(L0,L1),P(L2,L3)),P(P(L4,L5),L6),ROOT), inner P never ROOT.

## Literal successful carriers

Offsets below refer to payload in the encoded proof; file coordinates are separate.
H=header, P=one parent, S=group setup, K=one reducer kernel, E=EOF commit. Every
proper prefix of each action sequence must return no completed view. Output Parent
events consume one action each; output leaves borrow exactly the listed proof bytes.

## Admission, malformed proof, and precedence

| Case | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| Exhaust all 256 logs, including short/empty file | Geometry InvalidBlockSize outside host range; compile-only 32-bit evidence labeled separately. |
| Reversed range, EOF+1, both invalid log/range | Geometry InvalidRange; bad log takes precedence. |
| Nonempty file 0..0, interior empty, EOF empty | EmptyRequest before root/proof parsing. |
| Empty file valid 0..0 / invalid 0..1 | Four-step root verification / InvalidRange. |
| Expected root has p or MAX in each word independently | NonCanonicalRoot { word: 0/1/2/3 }; ascending first error, before header. |
| Root word 0 or p-1 | Canonical admission, then ordinary proof mismatch if not the matching root; no false validity claim. |
| Hello header declared 0, 4, 6, 4096, MAX, trusted length 5 | Header SizeMismatch with exact expected/declared. LE 5 succeeds. |
| Every header prefix 0..7 bytes | Truncated { offset:0, needed:8 }. |
| Every truncated prefix of three-interior, grouped-left | Stage-specific Truncated cursor/needed; complete bytes required before examining a pair or starting a group. |
| Pair word p/MAX, left and right, all four positions | NonCanonicalHash at 8+word*8 or 40+word*8 for first pair, before parent hashing; second parent starts at 72. |
| Noncanonical left followed by incomplete right pair | Pair Truncated takes precedence over canonical validation. |
| Canonical pair order swap, canonical wrong root, modified canonical sibling | ParentMismatch at literal node3 or1, before group output. |
| Three-boundary first/last payload mutation | LeafMismatch counter 0/1; completed earlier group never yields a view. |
| Same selected data computed at wrong absolute counter | LeafMismatch of selected start counter. |
| Single/multiple group suffix 1 byte / concatenated valid proof | TrailingBytes at exact original carrier length after hashing, no view. |
| Empty-file correct root + suffix / canonical wrong root | TrailingBytes offset 8 / LeafMismatch counter 0. |
| Nonempty file header-only, nonempty request | First parent or leaf setup Truncated, never success with an unchecked root. |
| u64MAX file, log 51 on 64-bit, first-byte query, short carrier | Bounded Parent processing then leaf Truncated with exact large needed; no allocation or overflowing end. |

The synthetic-canonical and four synthetic-alias fixtures bind a supplied parent
commitment with an arbitrary zero unselected sibling. Each alias changes only one
right child's LE word from 0 to p. Legacy accepts all under the same supplied root;
the new profile must reject each at offsets 40, 48, 56, 64. No actual complete file
with this synthetic root has been witnessed; these fixtures prove encoding behavior,
not a particle bypass or a complete-file collision.

## Work, state, retention, and bounds

- Exact valid action sequences above under per-call schedules 1,2,total,total+1;
  an external test budget with total exact and exact+1 completes at identical paid
  count; total one below prevents final commit and yields no authority. Radio has
  no WorkBudget constructor, so the harness must not pretend to test Kadek's adapter.
- Interleave two jobs and pause after every stage. No kernel repeats on resumption.
  Every error becomes sticky; repeat 10 failed polls returns identical variant/offset,
  no additional state work. Repeat 10 completed polls returns None. Drop at every
  pending stage performs no additional hashing or output.
- Rewalk each completed view twice, interleave its output cursors, assert exact
  event counts, slices, pointer offsets, file offsets and request intersections.
  Retain a borrowed view/leaf after verifier/cursor drop while proof stays alive.
  Empty view has 0 events and immediately complete. Final next makes complete true.
- Field/constructor seal compiler probes: a valid public API control succeeds;
  raw VerifiedProof struct literal fails E0451; no From/raw constructor/Deserialize
  escape exists; destroying or mutating borrowed proof storage before use fails
  the expected borrow-check class. Capture actual compiler diagnostics, not only
  generic compile_fail. No test claims aliases imply extra allocation charges.
- A host counting allocator test has an allocating positive control and observes
  constructor, each verifier stage, completed output replay, errors/terminal polls
  and cancellation/drop separately. Build and retain fixture storage outside observation; exclude its later deallocation. Concrete
  Poseidon2 scope only; a library cross compile does not prove allocator-free linking.
- Largest-depth fixed prefix uses u64MAX geometry, 52 explicit parent levels and
  one 4096-byte first leaf. Compute only those explicit synthetic commitments with
  zero deferred sibling CVs; a full-range request builds the 53-entry expected frontier
  then runs out of supplied proof. Observe Truncated and no result; do not enumerate
  the file or call this a real complete-file proof. This is separate from the already
  reviewed traversal boundary witness. Exact large coordinate/multiplication checks
  are asserted. Proof input retains no proof-sized output vector.

Canonical errors proposed Display strings (freeze with variants before coding):
geometry delegates to RangeTraversal; EmptyRequest = "empty request for nonempty file";
NonCanonicalRoot = "noncanonical expected root word {word}";
Truncated = "truncated proof at {offset}: need {needed} bytes";
SizeMismatch = "proof size {declared} differs from expected {expected}";
NonCanonicalHash = "noncanonical proof hash word at {offset}";
ParentMismatch = "proof parent mismatch at node {node_index}";
LeafMismatch = "proof leaf mismatch at chunk {start_chunk}";
TrailingBytes = "trailing proof bytes at {offset}".

# Bounded BAO proof research

Serves Kadek foundation row 4 through Radio (declared part of soft3) and a future Kadek adapter in Cyb. Stage: research complete;
PLAN proposed, not approved. Owned branch research/bao-bounded-proof; no production
changes, commit, push or vendor dispatch. Original preliminary note is retained in
`audit/2026-10-12-bao-proof-research/notes/initial-research.md`.

Source identities: Radio a96d86e28d384cdf656aadc4282c78c0c170c102; Hemera
d5f0a09539360934fb32db9b2e15a91995607db6; committed Kadek anchor
4d9ca9ab3498238b9c1e03f6d6f160e1ec729912. RangeTraversal merged as Radio PR39,
b933829cbe273d2f31fe75ca83125f3ddd8917a2. Ordinary fetch/fast-forward and an exact
source bridge confirm candidate identity and unchanged kernels/carrier/lock/consumers,
except module wiring. New implementation will use b933; all research measurements
remain attributed to a96d. No broad Radio matrix was run
for research. This research does not close Kadek row 4 or prove an allocator-free
final no_std application link.

## Existing carrier and measured boundary

`cyber-bao/src/io/slice.rs` writes eight LE complete-length bytes, selected preorder
parent pairs and complete selected groups. Multi-group verification allocates
traversal, expected-hash and output vectors, hashes an entire group in one call,
and does not require encoded EOF. The single-group shortcut hashes all remaining
bytes without checking their length against the header. Single-group extraction
includes the body even for an empty request; multi-group empty extraction is only
its header. These are existing API semantics, left unchanged by the proposed unit.

The immutable legacy probe manually supplies LE5 + hello, mutates only the header,
appends a six-byte suffix to a two-chunk carrier, and checks empty requests. At
Radio a96d and Rust 1.95, debug and release both accept declared lengths 0,4,5,6,4096
for five actual bytes; accept the suffix after an 8264-byte proof; and return an
empty result for an empty multi-group query with a wrong root. Both captured outputs
are identical 321-byte files. These are size/EOF/empty-selection admission witnesses,
not forged hashes, a particle/root substitution, or new-reader test failures.

Commands are captured verbatim in records/initial-debug.json and legacy-release.json:
`cargo run [--release] --locked --offline --manifest-path .../probe/Cargo.toml`,
Rust 1.95, jobs 2, incremental 0, RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings, a private target directory.
The legacy three-file freeze remains unchanged. Root's first inline source-freeze
helper used a wrong Ruby text/binary comparison and was corrected before handoff;
that helper mistake is not a production discrepancy.

## Independent carrier and encoding evidence

The separate 269-line primary-probe manually writes eight real-file carriers using
explicit small chunk/parent expressions. They cover empty/hello, three-chunk right
and interior selection, a cross-chunk request, grouped-left selection, a final
one-byte group and a sole two-chunk group. Its root/offset/action table is in
finite-cases.md. It compares with existing producer/reader only after construction;
roundtrip is supplemental, not the wire-shape oracle. Existing empty, hello,
D8192 ROOT and historical non-ROOT literals are asserted independently. New D12288
and D8193 roots are pinned kernel-expression results, not independent cryptographic
implementations. Printed action counts are proposed-machine literals, not measured
new-API work. Debug and release outputs and every generated fixture agree exactly.

At pinned Hemera, public `hemera::field::P` is 0xffff_ffff_0000_0001 and Hash stores
four LE words. `Hash::from_bytes` preserves any 32 bytes with bytewise equality.
`bytes_to_cv` calls Goldilocks::new, which stores raw u64; it does not itself reduce.
Field arithmetic and canonical hash output identify residue-equivalent values.
This corrects the preliminary shorthand that the constructor performs reduction.
The normative output encoding is canonical [0,p); parent input parsing currently
has no representation guard. The new profile must check all four words of root
and both children before hashing; it must not normalize an invalid encoding.

A synthetic parent commitment, with actual selected-left chunk CV and an arbitrary
zero unselected-right CV, demonstrates each word independently. Replacing any one
zero right word by p preserves parent output and legacy acceptance; the raw Hash
still differs. Five fixtures retain the canonical and four alias carriers. There
is no witnessed complete file for this synthetic commitment. This measures encoding
behavior, not a complete-file collision or a Kadek authority bypass. Separate
0,p-1,p,MAX raw-word controls retain their exact bytes. The proposed canonical rule
accepts the first two representations and rejects the latter two, regardless of any
later root mismatch. All checks pass in debug/release with strict warnings.

## Smallest composition

GroupHasher is already merged: one <=4096-byte leaf or one parent kernel per call,
fixed 53 frontier, absolute counters and only final ROOT. Merged RangeTraversal
supplies checked u64/usize geometry with a fixed 53-frame frontier and one selected
node per call. Parent child flags specify which paths to visit; both child hashes
remain necessary. Neither primitive parses the carrier or binds a particle.

Proposed composition is concrete Poseidon2: a borrowed whole-buffer verifier,
fixed expected-hash stack and at most one borrowed GroupHasher. Generic HashBackend
permits arbitrary allocation/cost through its associated Hash and methods, so it
cannot justify unconditional resource guarantees. No new hash arithmetic, generic
transform, allocation, byte owner or budget abstraction is needed. The verifier
checks trusted header length, all parents/groups, canonical words and exact EOF,
then seals a borrowed result. Its output cursor emits one parent event or whole
leaf slice per call; it never scans for a leaf, hashes again, or allocates a span
vector. Kadek will separately match its BaoAnchor and own quota/pool/copy/publication.

For a valid nonempty proof, exact action count is header 1 + parents P + group setups L
+ sum(2*N_group-1) + EOF 1 =2+P+2*C; empty file costs 4 and has no output items.
No exact total is computed by an uncharged traversal. Caller-supplied persistent
budgets can stop before any next action without publication. One atomic kernel
remains a bounded unit, not constant wall time. Immutable proof borrowing prevents
mutation after verification; completed views depend on proof lifetime, not verifier
lifetime. Private fields prevent fabricating a completed view through safe API.

Chosen empty policy is strict: reject any empty request to a nonempty file, including
EOF, instead of adding a successful no-authentication mode. Empty file 0..0 still
hashes canonical empty ROOT and requires an exact eight-byte header. This API is a
new strict profile; unchanged legacy single-group empty extraction does not define
its admission. A profile-preserving grouping change uses repaired canonical roots;
old grouped internal CVs are not grandfathered.

## Consumers, scope and remaining verification

Within exact Radio a96d, four manifests directly depend on cyber-bao: iroh-blobs,
particle, radio-cli, tests/integration. iroh-docs depends on local iroh-blobs. Current
particle/radio-cli use std encode/decode/outboard; the iroh-blobs slice decoder call
is in the cfg(test) export_pairs module. Other current slice callers are cyber-bao
unit/integration tests. The new additive module migrates none of those APIs. Radio
exporter writes the same size/pair carrier; its actual literal exporter tests are
relevant regression coverage. Sync/fsm transport variants can omit a size header;
they are not silently admitted as this complete-buffer profile.

Kadek now pins cyber-bao a96d with default-features=false for BaoAnchor, using the
same merged GroupHasher and Git Hemera d5; its plain particle remains registry
Hemera 0.3.1. Same immutable-body association is the authority invariant, not alleged
identity of the two source packages. A Radio result verified under an arbitrary
root cannot mint VerifiedRange. The future adapter remains a separate reviewed unit.

The prior exact-revision graph inventory is retained by hash/reference, with its
cached-default and retained-Nu distinctions; it is not a fresh network inventory.
The registry declares Radio part_of soft3 and Kadek part_of Cyb. New public API
reaches ring 1 even when existing direct calls remain unchanged. Required closure/gates and unavailable
platforms must be reported explicitly. No inherited-red assumption is attached to
this new candidate: eventual negatives need current exact-parent comparisons.

Next: freeze the bounded fresh vendor PLAN input after root's draft read and the
completed traversal merge bridge. Only approval authorizes tests-first
production. Implementation will supply exact diagnostics, allocation and lifetime
witnesses, current owner/workspace/consumer gates and a separate fresh CODE review.

===== 046 BEGIN probe/src/main.rs | 2322 bytes | SHA256 5a6f0b73072d4d40075dbe57c26408a52a3634e898036eb452da0fe54436f526 =====
//! Characterize the committed legacy carrier, without proposing a new decoder.
use cyber_bao::{
    BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, ChunkNum, ChunkRanges, HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend,
    io::slice::{decode_slice, extract_slice},
};

fn main() {
    let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
    let hello = b"hello";
    let (root, proof) = extract_slice(&backend, hello, 0..5, BlockSize::ZERO);
    assert_eq!(proof, [5_u64.to_le_bytes().as_slice(), hello].concat());
    let one = ChunkRanges::from(ChunkNum(0)..ChunkNum(1));
    for declared in [0_u64, 4, 5, 6, 4096] {
        let mut changed = proof.clone();
        changed[..8].copy_from_slice(&declared.to_le_bytes());
        let decoded = decode_slice(&backend, &changed, &root, &one, BlockSize::ZERO)
            .expect("legacy single-group decoder accepts changed header");
        assert_eq!(decoded, vec![(0, hello.to_vec())]);
        println!("single declared={declared} payload=5 accepted=true");
    }

    let body = vec![0x42; 2 * CHUNK_SIZE];
    let all = ChunkRanges::from(ChunkNum(0)..ChunkNum(2));
    let (root, mut proof) = extract_slice(&backend, &body, 0..8192, BlockSize::ZERO);
    let original_len = proof.len();
    proof.extend_from_slice(b"suffix");
    let decoded = decode_slice(&backend, &proof, &root, &all, BlockSize::ZERO)
        .expect("legacy multi-group decoder accepts suffix");
    assert_eq!(
        decoded,
        vec![(0, body[..4096].to_vec()), (4096, body[4096..].to_vec())]
    );
    println!("multi original={original_len} suffix=6 accepted=true");

    let (_, single_empty) = extract_slice(&backend, hello, 2..2, BlockSize::ZERO);
    assert_eq!(
        single_empty,
        [5_u64.to_le_bytes().as_slice(), hello].concat()
    );
    let (_, multi_empty) = extract_slice(&backend, &body, 2..2, BlockSize::ZERO);
    assert_eq!(multi_empty, 8192_u64.to_le_bytes());
    let empty = ChunkRanges::from(ChunkNum(0)..ChunkNum(0));
    let wrong = backend.chunk_hash(b"wrong", 0, true);
    let decoded = decode_slice(&backend, &multi_empty, &wrong, &empty, BlockSize::ZERO)
        .expect("legacy empty traversal performs no root check");
    assert!(decoded.is_empty());
    println!(
        "empty single_wire={} multi_wire={} multi_wrong_root_accepted=true",
        single_empty.len(),
        multi_empty.len()
    );
}

===== 047 BEGIN primary-probe/src/main.rs | 7858 bytes | SHA256 6c274529c97ebc1e98ffd0bcb37e2e47abf02f5e1db7ca07480ff20ea432971f =====
//! Primary expressions and manual carriers, not a proposed verifier prototype.
use cyber_bao::{
    BlockSize, ChunkNum, ChunkRanges, HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend,
    group::GroupHasher,
    io::slice::{decode_slice, extract_slice},
};
use hemera::{Hash, field::P};
use std::{ops::Range, path::Path};

fn data(n: usize) -> Vec<u8> {
    (0..n).map(|i| ((31 * i + i / 4096) % 256) as u8).collect()
}
fn leaf(data: &[u8], index: u64) -> Hash {
    Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(data, index, false)
}
fn parent(left: Hash, right: Hash, root: bool) -> Hash {
    Poseidon2Backend.parent_hash(&left, &right, root)
}
fn pair(wire: &mut Vec<u8>, left: Hash, right: Hash) {
    wire.extend_from_slice(left.as_bytes());
    wire.extend_from_slice(right.as_bytes());
}
fn save(dir: &Path, name: &str, wire: &[u8]) {
    std::fs::write(dir.join(format!("{name}.bin")), wire).unwrap();
}
fn ranges(range: Range<u64>) -> ChunkRanges {
    ChunkRanges::from(ChunkNum(range.start / 4096)..ChunkNum(range.end.div_ceil(4096)))
}
#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
fn witness(
    dir: &Path,
    name: &str,
    body: &[u8],
    root: Hash,
    wire: &[u8],
    request: Range<u64>,
    log: u8,
    expected: &[(u64, Vec<u8>)],
    actions: u64,
) {
    assert_eq!(
        u64::from_le_bytes(wire[..8].try_into().unwrap()),
        u64::try_from(body.len()).unwrap()
    );
    assert_eq!(
        decode_slice(
            &Poseidon2Backend,
            wire,
            &root,
            &ranges(request.clone()),
            BlockSize(log)
        )
        .unwrap(),
        expected
    );
    // Producer agreement is supplemental. The wire above was built by literal expressions.
    assert_eq!(
        extract_slice(&Poseidon2Backend, body, request.clone(), BlockSize(log)),
        (root, wire.to_vec())
    );
    save(dir, name, wire);
    println!(
        "fixture={name} size={} log={log} request={}..{} wire={} root={root} expected_actions={actions}",
        body.len(),
        request.start,
        request.end,
        wire.len()
    );
}
fn main() {
    let dir = std::env::args_os().nth(1).expect("fixture directory");
    let dir = Path::new(&dir);
    assert_eq!(P, 0xffff_ffff_0000_0001);
    let empty = Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(&[], 0, true);
    let hello = Poseidon2Backend.chunk_hash(b"hello", 0, true);
    assert_eq!(
        empty.to_string(),
        "ea57b2e6b1ec7d2de11b15cb6d7060dd61d247fe0fbf5f7d3fb97a7be9328552"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        hello.to_string(),
        "626fa46e4e7bd5c87d630eef8333931a0b9198587400a0191eae7821692880d7"
    );
    save(dir, "empty", &0u64.to_le_bytes());
    let mut wire = 5u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    wire.extend_from_slice(b"hello");
    witness(
        dir,
        "hello",
        b"hello",
        hello,
        &wire,
        1..4,
        0,
        &[(0, b"hello".to_vec())],
        4,
    );
    println!("fixture=empty size=0 log=0 request=0..0 wire=8 root={empty} expected_actions=4");

    let body = data(12288);
    let l0 = leaf(&body[..4096], 0);
    let l1 = leaf(&body[4096..8192], 1);
    let l2 = leaf(&body[8192..], 2);
    let p01 = parent(l0, l1, false);
    let root = parent(p01, l2, true);
    let mut wire = 12288u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut wire, p01, l2);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[8192..]);
    witness(
        dir,
        "three-right",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        8192..8193,
        0,
        &[(8192, body[8192..].to_vec())],
        5,
    );
    let mut wire = 12288u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut wire, p01, l2);
    pair(&mut wire, l0, l1);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[4096..8192]);
    witness(
        dir,
        "three-interior",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        4097..4100,
        0,
        &[(4096, body[4096..8192].to_vec())],
        6,
    );
    let mut wire = 12288u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut wire, p01, l2);
    pair(&mut wire, l0, l1);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[..8192]);
    witness(
        dir,
        "three-boundary",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        4095..4097,
        0,
        &[
            (0, body[..4096].to_vec()),
            (4096, body[4096..8192].to_vec()),
        ],
        8,
    );
    let mut wire = 12288u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut wire, p01, l2);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[..8192]);
    witness(
        dir,
        "grouped-left",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        4095..4097,
        1,
        &[(0, body[..8192].to_vec())],
        7,
    );

    let body = data(8193);
    let l2 = leaf(&body[8192..], 2);
    let root = parent(p01, l2, true);
    let mut wire = 8193u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut wire, p01, l2);
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body[8192..]);
    witness(
        dir,
        "final-short",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        8192..8193,
        1,
        &[(8192, body[8192..].to_vec())],
        5,
    );

    let body = data(8192);
    let root = parent(l0, l1, true);
    assert_eq!(
        root.to_string(),
        "2abf65dd26465a7352707d79d18ff3aab9ee5d97614a774619c79ed527e20190"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        p01.to_string(),
        "11890a7e7cf1e4de463a04bf53bfdf269d627983a221daaf9029a3ff43297389"
    );
    assert_ne!(root, p01);
    let mut wire = 8192u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    wire.extend_from_slice(&body);
    witness(
        dir,
        "sole-group",
        &body,
        root,
        &wire,
        1..8191,
        1,
        &[(0, body.clone())],
        6,
    );
    let mut reducer = GroupHasher::new(&Poseidon2Backend, &body, 0, true).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(reducer.step(), None);
    assert_eq!(reducer.step(), None);
    assert_eq!(reducer.step(), Some(root));
    assert!(reducer.is_complete());
    assert_eq!(reducer.step(), None);

    // Synthetic parent commitment, NOT the root of a witnessed complete file.
    // Its unselected sibling is an arbitrary zero CV. This probes representation.
    let zero = Hash::from_bytes([0; 32]);
    let synthetic_root = parent(l0, zero, true);
    let mut canonical = 8192u64.to_le_bytes().to_vec();
    pair(&mut canonical, l0, zero);
    canonical.extend_from_slice(&body[..4096]);
    let query = ranges(0..1);
    let baseline = decode_slice(
        &Poseidon2Backend,
        &canonical,
        &synthetic_root,
        &query,
        BlockSize(0),
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(baseline, [(0, body[..4096].to_vec())]);
    save(dir, "synthetic-canonical", &canonical);
    for word in 0..4 {
        let mut noncanonical = [0; 32];
        noncanonical[word * 8..word * 8 + 8].copy_from_slice(&P.to_le_bytes());
        let raw = Hash::from_bytes(noncanonical);
        assert_ne!(raw, zero);
        assert_eq!(raw.as_bytes(), &noncanonical);
        assert_eq!(parent(l0, raw, true), synthetic_root);
        let mut alias = canonical.clone();
        alias[40 + word * 8..48 + word * 8].copy_from_slice(&P.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(
            decode_slice(
                &Poseidon2Backend,
                &alias,
                &synthetic_root,
                &query,
                BlockSize(0)
            )
            .unwrap(),
            baseline
        );
        save(dir, &format!("synthetic-alias-{word}"), &alias);
        println!(
            "alias_word={word} raw_equal=false parent_equal=true legacy_accept=true root={synthetic_root}"
        );
    }
    for value in [0, P - 1, P, u64::MAX] {
        let mut raw = [0; 32];
        raw[..8].copy_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
        assert_eq!(Hash::from_bytes(raw).as_bytes(), &raw);
        println!("word={value} proposed_canonical={}", value < P);
    }
    println!(
        "No new verifier API, allocation guarantee, or whole-file synthetic-root authority is tested here."
    );
}

//! Poseidon2-BAO: verified streaming with Poseidon2 hash over Goldilocks.
//!
//! This crate provides BAO-style content-verified streaming using the
//! Poseidon2 algebraic hash function. The tree structure
//! and encoding formats are compatible with the original bao specification,
//! differing only in the hash function used.
//!
//! # Architecture
//!
//! - **`tree`**: Pure geometry — tree node indexing, chunk counting, block sizes
//! - **`hash`**: Pluggable hash backend trait with Poseidon2 implementation
//! - **`io`**: Encoding, decoding, outboard creation, and slice extraction

#![cfg_attr(not(feature = "std"), no_std)]

pub mod group;
pub mod hash;
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub mod io;
pub mod traversal;
pub mod tree;

pub use hash::{HashBackend, Poseidon2Backend};
pub use tree::{BaoChunk, BaoTree, BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, ChunkNum, TreeNode};

#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub use tree::PostOrderChunkIter;

/// A set of chunk ranges (used for partial downloads, range requests, etc.).
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ChunkRanges = range_collections::RangeSet2<ChunkNum>;

/// Borrowed reference to chunk ranges.
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ChunkRangesRef = range_collections::RangeSetRef<ChunkNum>;

/// A set of byte ranges.
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub type ByteRanges = range_collections::RangeSet2<u64>;

===== 051 BEGIN Radio cyber-bao/src/group.rs | 6349 bytes | SHA256 948307e945b1f7f3808b5828dacb04547a89c701c8f00ba1534fa396d0b66cdf =====
//! Incremental fixed-chunk group hashing with a fixed frontier.
//!
//! This computes a hash only; it does not authenticate data or a particle/root
//! association. Each pending step calls one backend kernel, on at most 4096 input
//! bytes or two child hashes. Arbitrary backend code and hash destructors retain
//! their own cost and allocation behavior.

use core::fmt;

use crate::{CHUNK_SIZE, HashBackend};

/// A group cannot be represented by the backend's absolute chunk counters.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum GroupHashError {
    /// The input length does not fit in a u64 (unreachable on <=64-bit targets).
    InputTooLarge,
    /// The last absolute chunk counter would exceed u64::MAX.
    CounterOverflow,
}

impl fmt::Display for GroupHashError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(match self {
            Self::InputTooLarge => "group input length exceeds u64",
            Self::CounterOverflow => "group chunk counter overflows u64",
        })
    }
}

impl core::error::Error for GroupHashError {}

fn leaf_count(len: u64) -> u64 {
    (len / CHUNK_SIZE as u64 + u64::from(!len.is_multiple_of(CHUNK_SIZE as u64))).max(1)
}

fn validate_counter(len: u64, start: u64) -> Result<u64, GroupHashError> {
    let leaves = leaf_count(len);
    start
        .checked_add(leaves - 1)
        .ok_or(GroupHashError::CounterOverflow)?;
    Ok(leaves)
}

/// A resumable complete-left binary reduction over fixed 4096-byte chunks.
///
/// Construction and completed polls call no backend method. Every pending
/// [`step`](Self::step) performs exactly one leaf or parent kernel: for N chunks
/// (at least one, even for empty input), completion takes exactly 2*N-1 steps.
/// Only the completing kernel receives the requested root flag.
///
/// The caller owns scheduling: check [`is_complete`](Self::is_complete), admit
/// one unit from its persistent budget, then step. Intermediate steps return
/// `None` while `is_complete` is false; count kernel entries, not returned hashes.
/// No internal quota is renewed by polling. Backend panics have no recovery
/// contract. Hashes are moved, never cloned.
///
/// The frontier uses 53 slots: a u64 byte length needs at most 2^52 leaves;
/// before a leaf there are at most 52 complete peaks, plus the incoming leaf.
/// This fixed storage does not require allocation or a placeholder hash.
pub struct GroupHasher<'a, B: HashBackend> {
    backend: &'a B,
    remaining: &'a [u8],
    next_counter: u64,
    leaves: u64,
    processed: u64,
    frontier: [Option<B::Hash>; 53],
    occupied: usize,
    reductions: u32,
    root: bool,
    complete: bool,
}

impl<B: HashBackend> fmt::Debug for GroupHasher<'_, B> {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("GroupHasher")
            .field("remaining_bytes", &self.remaining.len())
            .field("processed_leaves", &self.processed)
            .field("leaves", &self.leaves)
            .field("occupied", &self.occupied)
            .field("is_complete", &self.complete)
            .finish_non_exhaustive()
    }
}

impl<'a, B: HashBackend> GroupHasher<'a, B> {
    /// Borrow a group and validate every counter before any hashing.
    ///
    /// Empty input is a single empty leaf. A sole leaf at u64::MAX is valid;
    /// two leaves starting there return [`GroupHashError::CounterOverflow`].
    pub fn new(
        backend: &'a B,
        data: &'a [u8],
        start_chunk: u64,
        is_root: bool,
    ) -> Result<Self, GroupHashError> {
        let len = u64::try_from(data.len()).map_err(|_| GroupHashError::InputTooLarge)?;
        let leaves = validate_counter(len, start_chunk)?;
        Ok(Self {
            backend,
            remaining: data,
            next_counter: start_chunk,
            leaves,
            processed: 0,
            frontier: core::array::from_fn(|_| None),
            occupied: 0,
            reductions: 0,
            root: is_root,
            complete: false,
        })
    }

    /// Whether the final hash has already been returned.
    pub fn is_complete(&self) -> bool {
        self.complete
    }

    /// Perform one pending kernel and return the hash only on the final step.
    ///
    /// Intermediate steps return None with `is_complete() == false`. Completed
    /// polls return None with `is_complete() == true` and perform no kernel.
    pub fn step(&mut self) -> Option<B::Hash> {
        if self.complete {
            return None;
        }
        if self.reductions != 0 {
            let right = self.frontier[self.occupied - 1]
                .take()
                .expect("occupied right peak");
            let left = self.frontier[self.occupied - 2]
                .take()
                .expect("occupied left peak");
            let final_parent = self.processed == self.leaves && self.occupied == 2;
            let hash = self
                .backend
                .parent_hash(&left, &right, self.root && final_parent);
            self.occupied -= 1;
            self.reductions -= 1;
            if final_parent {
                self.complete = true;
                return Some(hash);
            }
            self.frontier[self.occupied - 1] = Some(hash);
        } else {
            let take = self.remaining.len().min(CHUNK_SIZE);
            let hash = self.backend.chunk_hash(
                &self.remaining[..take],
                self.next_counter,
                self.root && self.leaves == 1,
            );
            self.remaining = &self.remaining[take..];
            self.processed += 1;
            if self.leaves == 1 {
                self.complete = true;
                return Some(hash);
            }
            self.frontier[self.occupied] = Some(hash);
            self.occupied += 1;
            if self.processed == self.leaves {
                // Fold remaining complete peaks from right to left.
                self.reductions = (self.occupied - 1) as u32;
            } else {
                // Merge equal-size complete peaks before accepting another leaf.
                self.reductions = self.processed.trailing_zeros();
                self.next_counter += 1; // all used counters were validated in new
            }
        }
        None
    }
}

===== 052 BEGIN Radio cyber-bao/src/hash.rs | 3960 bytes | SHA256 1e705848543784c326be3ecd347878eae8dc5ab0207392d64ba5d9efb695bf1f =====
//! Hash backend trait and Poseidon2 implementation.
//!
//! The `HashBackend` trait abstracts the hash function used in BAO tree
//! construction, making the tree machinery hash-agnostic. The `Poseidon2Backend`
//! provides the concrete implementation using hemera.

use hemera::{Hash, OUTPUT_BYTES};

/// Trait for pluggable hash functions in BAO tree operations.
///
/// Implementations must provide:
/// - Leaf hashing: hash a data chunk at a position in the file
/// - Parent hashing: combine two child hashes into a parent hash
pub trait HashBackend {
    /// The hash output type.
    type Hash: AsRef<[u8]> + Clone + Eq + core::fmt::Debug;

    /// Hash a data chunk (leaf node).
    ///
    /// - `data`: the chunk data
    /// - `counter`: 0-based position index in the file
    /// - `is_root`: true if this is the only chunk (root finalization)
    fn chunk_hash(&self, data: &[u8], counter: u64, is_root: bool) -> Self::Hash;

    /// Combine two child hashes into a parent hash.
    ///
    /// - `left`: left child hash
    /// - `right`: right child hash
    /// - `is_root`: true if this is the tree root
    fn parent_hash(&self, left: &Self::Hash, right: &Self::Hash, is_root: bool) -> Self::Hash;

    /// Size of the hash output in bytes.
    fn hash_size(&self) -> usize;

    /// A zero hash (used for empty/placeholder nodes).
    fn zero_hash(&self) -> Self::Hash;

    /// Construct a hash from its raw byte representation.
    fn hash_from_bytes(&self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Self::Hash;
}

/// Poseidon2 hash backend using hemera.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
pub struct Poseidon2Backend;

impl HashBackend for Poseidon2Backend {
    type Hash = Hash;

    fn chunk_hash(&self, data: &[u8], counter: u64, is_root: bool) -> Hash {
        hemera::tree::chunk_cv(data, counter, is_root)
    }

    fn parent_hash(&self, left: &Hash, right: &Hash, is_root: bool) -> Hash {
        hemera::tree::parent_cv(left, right, is_root)
    }

    fn hash_size(&self) -> usize {
        OUTPUT_BYTES
    }

    fn zero_hash(&self) -> Hash {
        Hash::from_bytes([0u8; OUTPUT_BYTES])
    }

    fn hash_from_bytes(&self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Hash {
        let mut arr = [0u8; OUTPUT_BYTES];
        arr.copy_from_slice(&bytes[..OUTPUT_BYTES]);
        Hash::from_bytes(arr)
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn poseidon2_backend_basic() {
        let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
        let h1 = backend.chunk_hash(b"hello", 0, false);
        let h2 = backend.chunk_hash(b"hello", 0, false);
        assert_eq!(h1, h2); // deterministic

        let h3 = backend.chunk_hash(b"world", 0, false);
        assert_ne!(h1, h3); // different data
    }

    #[test]
    fn poseidon2_backend_counter() {
        let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
        let h0 = backend.chunk_hash(b"data", 0, false);
        let h1 = backend.chunk_hash(b"data", 1, false);
        assert_ne!(h0, h1); // different counter
    }

    #[test]
    fn poseidon2_backend_parent() {
        let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
        let left = backend.chunk_hash(b"left", 0, false);
        let right = backend.chunk_hash(b"right", 1, false);
        let parent = backend.parent_hash(&left, &right, false);
        assert_ne!(parent, left);
        assert_ne!(parent, right);

        // Non-commutative
        let parent_rev = backend.parent_hash(&right, &left, false);
        assert_ne!(parent, parent_rev);
    }

    #[test]
    fn poseidon2_backend_root_differs() {
        let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
        let left = backend.chunk_hash(b"left", 0, false);
        let right = backend.chunk_hash(b"right", 1, false);
        let non_root = backend.parent_hash(&left, &right, false);
        let root = backend.parent_hash(&left, &right, true);
        assert_ne!(non_root, root);
    }

    #[test]
    fn hash_size() {
        let backend = Poseidon2Backend;
        assert_eq!(backend.hash_size(), OUTPUT_BYTES);
    }
}

===== 053 BEGIN Radio cyber-bao/src/tree.rs | 12320 bytes | SHA256 458f26235aee22c42376dde8929a55831c1f8a58dd59a5dac270cd9bb8bd667a =====
//! BAO tree geometry — pure math, no hashing.
//!
//! This module provides the tree structure used for verified streaming.
//! The tree uses in-order indexing (same as bao-tree): leaves at
//! even positions, parents at odd positions, level = trailing ones count.

use core::fmt;
use core::ops::Range;

use hemera::OUTPUT_BYTES;

#[cfg(feature = "std")]
mod traversal;
#[cfg(feature = "std")]
pub use traversal::PostOrderChunkIter;

/// Size of a single chunk in bytes (Hemera spec: 4 KiB).
pub const CHUNK_SIZE: usize = 4096;

/// Size of a hash pair (two hashes concatenated).
const PAIR_SIZE: usize = OUTPUT_BYTES * 2;

/// A node position in the in-order binary tree.
///
/// Leaves are at even indices (0, 2, 4, ...).
/// Parents are at odd indices with level = trailing_ones count.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]
pub struct TreeNode(pub(crate) u64);

impl TreeNode {
    /// Create a tree node from a raw in-order index.
    pub fn new(index: u64) -> Self {
        Self(index)
    }

    /// The level of this node (0 for leaves).
    pub fn level(self) -> u32 {
        self.0.trailing_ones()
    }

    /// Whether this is a leaf node.
    pub fn is_leaf(self) -> bool {
        (self.0 & 1) == 0
    }

    /// The midpoint chunk number (boundary between left and right subtrees).
    pub fn mid(self) -> ChunkNum {
        ChunkNum(self.0 + 1)
    }

    /// The left child of this node, if it has one.
    pub fn left_child(self) -> Option<Self> {
        let level = self.level();
        if level == 0 {
            return None;
        }
        Some(Self(self.0 - (1 << (level - 1))))
    }

    /// The right child of this node, if it has one.
    pub fn right_child(self) -> Option<Self> {
        let level = self.level();
        if level == 0 {
            return None;
        }
        Some(Self(self.0 + (1 << (level - 1))))
    }

    /// The parent of this node.
    pub fn parent(self) -> Option<Self> {
        let level = self.level();
        let span = 1u64 << level;
        let offset = self.0;
        if offset & (span << 1) == 0 {
            Some(Self(offset + span))
        } else {
            offset.checked_sub(span).map(Self)
        }
    }

    /// Range of chunks covered by this node's subtree.
    pub fn chunk_range(self) -> Range<ChunkNum> {
        let level = self.level();
        let span = 1u64 << level;
        let mid = self.0 + 1;
        ChunkNum(mid - span)..ChunkNum(mid + span)
    }

    /// Range of bytes covered by this node's subtree.
    pub fn byte_range(self) -> Range<u64> {
        let range = self.chunk_range();
        range.start.to_bytes()..range.end.to_bytes()
    }

    /// The right descendant of this node that is within the tree bounds.
    ///
    /// Starts at right child, then walks left until within `len`.
    pub fn right_descendant(self, len: Self) -> Option<Self> {
        let mut node = self.right_child()?;
        while node >= len {
            node = node.left_child()?;
        }
        Some(node)
    }

    /// Count of nodes below this node in the subtree (excluding self).
    pub fn count_below(self) -> u64 {
        let level = self.level();
        if level == 0 {
            return 0;
        }
        (1u64 << (level + 1)) - 2
    }

    /// Post-order offset of this node (closed-form).
    pub fn post_order_offset(self) -> u64 {
        let level = self.level();
        let span = 1u64 << level;
        let mid = self.0 + 1;
        let start = mid - span;
        // In a complete binary tree, post-order offset = start + count_below
        start + self.count_below()
    }

    /// Shift the node index by removing the block_size lowest bits.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if the node's level is below the block size
    /// (i.e., it's an intra-block node that doesn't appear in the block-level tree).
    pub const fn add_block_size(self, n: u8) -> Option<Self> {
        let mask = (1u64 << n) - 1;
        if self.0 & mask == mask {
            Some(Self(self.0 >> n))
        } else {
            None
        }
    }

    /// The inverse of `add_block_size` — expand back to the unshifted index.
    pub const fn subtract_block_size(self, n: u8) -> Self {
        let shifted = !(!self.0 << n);
        Self(shifted)
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for TreeNode {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "TreeNode({}, level={})", self.0, self.level())
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for TreeNode {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.0)
    }
}

/// Count of chunks (each CHUNK_SIZE bytes).
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Default)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]
pub struct ChunkNum(pub u64);

impl ChunkNum {
    /// Number of chunks needed for `size` bytes (rounds up).
    pub fn chunks(size: u64) -> Self {
        Self(size.div_ceil(CHUNK_SIZE as u64))
    }

    /// Number of full chunks in `size` bytes (rounds down).
    pub fn full_chunks(size: u64) -> Self {
        Self(size / CHUNK_SIZE as u64)
    }

    /// Convert chunks back to bytes.
    pub fn to_bytes(self) -> u64 {
        self.0 * CHUNK_SIZE as u64
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for ChunkNum {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "ChunkNum({})", self.0)
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for ChunkNum {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}", self.0)
    }
}

impl core::ops::Add for ChunkNum {
    type Output = Self;
    fn add(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 + rhs.0)
    }
}

impl core::ops::Add<u64> for ChunkNum {
    type Output = Self;
    fn add(self, rhs: u64) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 + rhs)
    }
}

impl core::ops::Sub for ChunkNum {
    type Output = Self;
    fn sub(self, rhs: Self) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 - rhs.0)
    }
}

impl core::ops::Sub<u64> for ChunkNum {
    type Output = Self;
    fn sub(self, rhs: u64) -> Self {
        Self(self.0 - rhs)
    }
}

#[cfg(feature = "std")]
impl range_collections::range_set::RangeSetEntry for ChunkNum {
    fn min_value() -> Self {
        ChunkNum(0)
    }

    fn is_min_value(&self) -> bool {
        self.0 == 0
    }
}

/// Block size for chunk groups — log2 of the number of chunks per block.
///
/// `BlockSize(0)` = CHUNK_SIZE bytes (one chunk per block).
/// `BlockSize(2)` = 4 × CHUNK_SIZE bytes (4 chunks per block, recommended default).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct BlockSize(pub u8);

impl BlockSize {
    /// One chunk per block.
    pub const ZERO: Self = Self(0);

    /// Default 16 KiB blocks (4 × 4096-byte chunks, recommended for production).
    pub const DEFAULT: Self = Self(2);

    /// Create from the log2 of chunks per block.
    pub const fn from_chunk_log(log: u8) -> Self {
        Self(log)
    }

    /// Create from a byte size (must be a power of 2 >= CHUNK_SIZE).
    pub fn from_bytes(bytes: u64) -> Option<Self> {
        if bytes < CHUNK_SIZE as u64 || !bytes.is_power_of_two() {
            return None;
        }
        let log = (bytes / CHUNK_SIZE as u64).trailing_zeros() as u8;
        Some(Self(log))
    }

    /// Block size in bytes.
    pub fn bytes(self) -> usize {
        CHUNK_SIZE << self.0
    }

    /// Log2 of chunks per block.
    pub fn chunk_log(self) -> u8 {
        self.0
    }

    /// Log2 of chunks per block as u32.
    pub fn to_u32(self) -> u32 {
        self.0 as u32
    }
}

/// The BAO tree geometry descriptor.
///
/// Contains no actual data or hashes — purely describes the tree shape
/// for a blob of a given size and block size.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct BaoTree {
    size: u64,
    block_size: BlockSize,
}

impl BaoTree {
    /// Create a new tree for the given data size and block size.
    pub fn new(size: u64, block_size: BlockSize) -> Self {
        Self { size, block_size }
    }

    /// Total data size in bytes.
    pub fn size(&self) -> u64 {
        self.size
    }

    /// Block size configuration.
    pub fn block_size(&self) -> BlockSize {
        self.block_size
    }

    /// Number of chunks (CHUNK_SIZE bytes each).
    pub fn chunks(&self) -> ChunkNum {
        ChunkNum::chunks(self.size)
    }

    /// Number of blocks (chunk groups). Minimum 1 even for empty data.
    pub fn blocks(&self) -> u64 {
        let chunk_count = self.chunks().0;
        let chunks_per_block = 1u64 << self.block_size.0;
        chunk_count.div_ceil(chunks_per_block).max(1)
    }

    /// Root node of the tree (at the block level).
    ///
    /// In the in-order binary tree, the root of N leaves is at
    /// index `next_power_of_two(N) - 1`.
    pub fn root(&self) -> TreeNode {
        let blocks = self.blocks();
        if blocks <= 1 {
            return TreeNode(0);
        }
        TreeNode(blocks.next_power_of_two() - 1)
    }

    /// Size of the outboard (hash pairs only, no size prefix).
    /// Each internal node is a hash pair (two OUTPUT_BYTES hashes).
    pub fn outboard_size(&self) -> u64 {
        let blocks = self.blocks();
        if blocks <= 1 {
            return 0;
        }
        (blocks - 1) * PAIR_SIZE as u64
    }

    /// Block size in bytes (chunk group size).
    pub fn chunk_group_bytes(&self) -> usize {
        self.block_size.bytes()
    }

    /// Compute the shifted tree representation (block-level tree).
    ///
    /// Returns `(root_node, filled_size)` in the shifted coordinate system.
    pub fn shifted(&self) -> (TreeNode, TreeNode) {
        let level = self.block_size.0;
        let size = self.size;
        let shift = CHUNK_SIZE.trailing_zeros() + level as u32;
        let mask = (1u64 << shift) - 1;
        let full_blocks = size >> shift;
        let open_block = u64::from((size & mask) != 0);
        let blocks = (full_blocks + open_block).max(1);
        let n = blocks.div_ceil(2);
        let root = n.next_power_of_two() - 1;
        let filled_size = n + n.saturating_sub(1);
        (TreeNode(root), TreeNode(filled_size))
    }

    /// Compute the left, mid, and right byte boundaries for a leaf node.
    ///
    /// `(start, mid.min(size), end.min(size))`
    pub fn leaf_byte_ranges3(&self, node: TreeNode) -> (u64, u64, u64) {
        let Range { start, end } = node.byte_range();
        let mid = node.mid().to_bytes();
        (start, mid.min(self.size), end.min(self.size))
    }

    /// Convert a node's in-order chunk_range to actual chunk numbers.
    ///
    /// `TreeNode::chunk_range()` returns values in the in-order index space
    /// where leaf at index 2k covers range 2k..2k+2. Actual chunk numbers
    /// are: block_idx * chunks_per_block, where block_idx = leaf_index / 2.
    /// This method performs that conversion for use with `ChunkRanges`.
    pub fn node_actual_chunk_range(&self, node: TreeNode) -> Range<ChunkNum> {
        let raw = node.chunk_range();
        let cpb = 1u64 << self.block_size.0;
        // In-order index space → block space: divide by 2
        // Block space → chunk space: multiply by chunks_per_block
        ChunkNum((raw.start.0 / 2) * cpb)..ChunkNum((raw.end.0 / 2) * cpb)
    }

    /// Whether a node is relevant for the outboard (i.e., tracked at this block size).
    pub fn is_relevant_for_outboard(&self, node: TreeNode) -> bool {
        let level = node.level();
        let bs = self.block_size.to_u32();
        if level < bs {
            false
        } else if level > bs {
            true
        } else {
            node.mid().to_bytes() < self.size
        }
    }
}

/// Items yielded during tree traversal.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum BaoChunk {
    /// A parent node — contains two child hashes (PAIR_SIZE bytes).
    Parent {
        node: TreeNode,
        is_root: bool,
        /// Whether the left child will be visited in this traversal.
        left: bool,
        /// Whether the right child will be visited in this traversal.
        right: bool,
    },
    /// A leaf node — contains actual data.
    Leaf {
        start_chunk: u64,
        size: usize,
        is_root: bool,
    },
}

mod tests;

===== 054 BEGIN Radio cyber-bao/src/traversal.rs | 6067 bytes | SHA256 87f646ef36a1e7ba1be3dd4576acf4bf6637d1ec6705953aef0bf8c6ac42eb5a =====
//! Checked, allocation-free preorder geometry for one byte range.
//!
//! This cursor emits geometry only. It does not parse a proof or authenticate
//! a particle, root, payload or empty request.

use core::{fmt, iter::FusedIterator, ops::Range};

use crate::{BaoChunk, BlockSize, CHUNK_SIZE, TreeNode};

/// Invalid geometry supplied to [`RangeTraversal::new`].
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum RangeTraversalError {
    /// The group width cannot be represented by both u64 and usize.
    InvalidBlockSize,
    /// The byte range is reversed or extends beyond the file.
    InvalidRange,
}

impl fmt::Display for RangeTraversalError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(match self {
            Self::InvalidBlockSize => "invalid traversal block size",
            Self::InvalidRange => "invalid traversal byte range",
        })
    }
}

impl core::error::Error for RangeTraversalError {}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
struct Frame {
    first: u64,
    count: u64,
    is_root: bool,
}

/// Preorder cursor over whole groups intersecting a half-open byte range.
///
/// Construction uses fixed storage for 53 frames. Each pending [`Iterator::next`]
/// pops one frame, pushes at most two children and returns one item, without
/// allocation, recursion, scanning or hashing. Pausing requires no extra state:
/// simply retain the cursor without calling `next`.
///
/// ROOT belongs to the complete file tree, even when the request selects only
/// one leaf. Parent flags identify children visited by this request; a parent
/// still describes a pair of hashes. An empty request is immediately complete
/// and emits no item; it makes no authentication claim.
pub struct RangeTraversal {
    size: u64,
    group_bytes: u64,
    selected: Range<u64>,
    frames: [Frame; 53],
    pending: usize,
}

impl fmt::Debug for RangeTraversal {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("RangeTraversal")
            .field("size", &self.size)
            .field("group_bytes", &self.group_bytes)
            .field("selected", &self.selected)
            .field("pending", &self.pending)
            .finish()
    }
}

impl RangeTraversal {
    /// Admit a file size, group width and half-open requested byte interval.
    ///
    /// Block geometry is validated before the request: the block log must be at
    /// most 51, and its byte width must fit usize. This admits logs 0..=51 on
    /// 64-bit targets and 0..=19 on 32-bit targets, even for a short or empty file.
    /// Width admission concerns integer geometry, not allocation layout validity.
    /// The request must satisfy `start <= end <= size`; an empty interval at EOF
    /// is valid. Only the final file group is clipped to its actual byte length.
    pub fn new(
        size: u64,
        block_size: BlockSize,
        request: Range<u64>,
    ) -> Result<Self, RangeTraversalError> {
        if block_size.0 > 51 {
            return Err(RangeTraversalError::InvalidBlockSize);
        }
        let group_bytes = (CHUNK_SIZE as u64) << block_size.0;
        usize::try_from(group_bytes).map_err(|_| RangeTraversalError::InvalidBlockSize)?;
        if request.start > request.end || request.end > size {
            return Err(RangeTraversalError::InvalidRange);
        }
        let mut result = Self {
            size,
            group_bytes,
            selected: 0..0,
            frames: [Frame {
                first: 0,
                count: 0,
                is_root: false,
            }; 53],
            pending: 0,
        };
        if request.start < request.end {
            result.selected = request.start / group_bytes..(request.end - 1) / group_bytes + 1;
            result.push(Frame {
                first: 0,
                count: size.div_ceil(group_bytes),
                is_root: true,
            });
        }
        Ok(result)
    }

    /// Whether no item remains, including immediately after the last emission.
    pub fn is_complete(&self) -> bool {
        self.pending == 0
    }

    fn push(&mut self, frame: Frame) {
        // N <= 2^52: at depth d <= 52, at most d deferred siblings plus
        // one current frame exist. Pop before pushing children preserves 53.
        self.frames[self.pending] = frame;
        self.pending += 1;
    }
}

impl Iterator for RangeTraversal {
    type Item = BaoChunk;

    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        if self.is_complete() {
            return None;
        }
        self.pending -= 1;
        let frame = self.frames[self.pending];
        if frame.count == 1 {
            // first < N implies first * group_bytes <= size - 1. Subtract
            // before clipping: the next group's byte end need not fit u64.
            let start = frame.first * self.group_bytes;
            return Some(BaoChunk::Leaf {
                start_chunk: start / CHUNK_SIZE as u64,
                size: self.group_bytes.min(self.size - start) as usize,
                is_root: frame.is_root,
            });
        }
        let left_count = 1u64 << (frame.count - 1).ilog2();
        let middle = frame.first + left_count;
        let end = frame.first + frame.count;
        let left = self.selected.start < middle && frame.first < self.selected.end;
        let right = self.selected.start < end && middle < self.selected.end;
        if right {
            self.push(Frame {
                first: middle,
                count: frame.count - left_count,
                is_root: false,
            });
        }
        if left {
            self.push(Frame {
                first: frame.first,
                count: left_count,
                is_root: false,
            });
        }
        // middle < N <= 2^52, so this group-based index is below 2^53.
        Some(BaoChunk::Parent {
            node: TreeNode::new(2 * middle - 1),
            is_root: frame.is_root,
            left,
            right,
        })
    }
}

impl FusedIterator for RangeTraversal {}
